- it returns when a run ends without a crash, or after `max_boots` boots.

The state directory is in memory (`MemFs`) or on disk. Both publish `state.json` only when a run ends or, after a crash, from its capsule; the disk mode first published it after every event, about 300 MiB of renames for one default session, until [#868](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/868). `Replay::Warm` resumes from the crashed boot's capsule and `Replay::Cold` folds the journal again. Both modes verify the capsule against a fresh fold. Bench children keep the disk mode with a temporary directory ([ADR 0060](adr/0060-bench-children-keep-temporary-journals.md)). An observer sees every appended event and can stop the run (the session calls it through `RunInput::on_append`, once per event after it is durable, so it works with either persistence) or arm the simulator's faults; it replaces Go's `Until` and `Wrap` journal wrappers.

The crate also holds the tuner's simulation tests ([#741](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/741)): `forecast_sim_test.go` and `memo_test.go` become `tests/sim/forecast.rs` and `tests/sim/memo.rs`. They need whole simulated sessions. `togi-tuner` cannot take `togi-simrun` as a dev-dependency, because the cycle would build a second `togi-tuner` whose types do not unify with the first.

Size: about 750 lines of library, 200 lines of unit tests and 4,600 lines of integration tests. The crate's 80 tests run in about 22 s under `cargo nextest` on Linux. The memo recomputation and the default-machine memory-against-files checks take 15 to 21 s each.

- **Go bugs fixed.**
  - Cold-boot verification was a silent no-op; Rust verifies the capsule in both replay modes.
  - Memory mode wrote disk files.
  - A JSON round trip worked around aliasing.
  - A failed log flush was discarded. Rust flushes the caller's log when the simulation ends, and a flush failure is `SimError::FlushLog`.
  - A capped run was classified by matching `ErrBootCap` in the error chain. In Rust, a verification or publication failure on the last boot is an error and never `Capped`.
  - A later process rebuilt the machine from the journals alone and lost three things: the pending machine checks, the reset reason the next boot reports, and the time between the last event and the crash. A capped simulation now leaves `machine.json` (`togi_sim::Persisted`), and `resume_simulator` continues from it.
- **Defects found in other crates.**
  - `togi-journal` committed whole-millisecond `mono` to disk but kept nanoseconds in memory, so a capsule diverged after a reopen. Fixed in [#804](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/804).
  - An `OnFinish` run that crashed published no state document. Fixed with `ResumeCapsule::publish`.
  - An undecodable archive blocked carry selection. Fixed in `togi-session`.
- **Review fixes before merge.**
  - A crashed boot's other errors, such as a failed log write, are `SimError::Failed`; they were dropped.
  - The observer kept no events after the caller's log failed.
  - `machine.json` was removed before a continuation made progress.
  - An in-memory simulation's next session read the last trial's samples of the earlier session.
  - A persisted machine decoded from positional arrays.
- **`togi-sim` changes.** `Simulator::persisted`, `Simulator::restore` (objects only) and `Simulator::forget_last_trial`. `set_samples_dir` keeps the last trial's samples when the directory does not change.
- **Not ported.** Go's older-schema carry cases and the "known events carry past an unknown kind" case. Rust has one schema and refuses unknown kinds; the remaining property, that an undecodable archive is archived and carries nothing, is pinned. The table below gives the Rust tests for each Go test, or the reason a Go test has none.
- **Not measured.** Compile times, and the comparison with `go test ./internal/simrun`.

### Go tests and their Rust tests

Every Go test function in `internal/simrun/*_test.go`, `internal/tuner/memo_test.go` and `internal/tuner/forecast_sim_test.go` is listed below: 46 functions, and the 74 Rust tests that port them. Go paths are relative to `internal/simrun`, or to `internal` for the two tuner files. Rust names are nextest names. Integration tests are in the `sim` binary (`tests/sim/<module>.rs`), and `simulate::tests::` names are library unit tests.

| Go test | Rust tests | Notes |
|:---|:---|:---|
| `carry_test.go` `TestTransitionWithUnknownKinds` | `carry::a_journal_with_an_unknown_kind_is_refused_untouched_by_the_build_that_wrote_it`<br>`carry::an_older_journal_with_an_unknown_kind_is_archived_and_carries_nothing` | The current-build case maps to the first test; the older-ruleset case maps to the second test, which checks that the journal is archived and carries nothing. Not ported: the older-schema and schema-2 cases, because Rust has one schema and never reads or translates a Go journal. Also not ported: Go's carry of known events past an unknown kind, including the interrupted older-ruleset case, because Rust refuses unknown kinds and an undecodable archive supplies no carry evidence. |

| `simrun_test.go` `TestRecordedConfigWithoutJournalIsFresh` | `config::a_journal_without_a_recorded_configuration_gives_the_fresh_one` |  |
| `simrun_test.go` `TestFirstResultStopsAfterPhaseOnesPassedCycle` | `first_result::the_first_result_stops_after_phase_ones_passed_cycle` |  |
| `smu_matrix_test.go` `TestPowerLossDuringHuntResume` | `interrupt::power_loss_during_hunt_resume_0`<br>`interrupt::power_loss_during_hunt_resume_1`<br>`interrupt::power_loss_during_hunt_resume_2`<br>`interrupt::power_loss_during_hunt_resume_3` | Together the four tests interrupt at every durable append and every SMU write of Go's matrix: test `_i` takes every fourth point, starting at point `i`. |
| `smu_matrix_test.go` `TestPowerLossDuringDeepeningResume` | `interrupt::power_loss_during_deepening_resume_0`<br>`interrupt::power_loss_during_deepening_resume_1`<br>`interrupt::power_loss_during_deepening_resume_2`<br>`interrupt::power_loss_during_deepening_resume_3` | Together the four tests interrupt at every durable append and every SMU write of Go's matrix: test `_i` takes every fourth point, starting at point `i`. |
| `tuner/memo_test.go` `TestMemosMatchRecomputation` | `memo::flat_hazard_7`<br>`memo::target_shared_voltage_2`<br>`memo::target_r7_request_gap_1`<br>`memo::misleading_mce_3`<br>`memo::late_onset_1` | The same five machine, seed and boot cases. Each checks full state and projection equality every 64 events and at the end, and that `next` agrees at each asked boundary. Go's memo epochs are obsolete: Rust queries are read-only and fold work into the event that changes its input. |
| `tuner/forecast_sim_test.go` `TestForecastMatchesSimulatedTrials` | `forecast::seed_1_two_cores`<br>`forecast::seed_7_two_cores_crash_joint`<br>`forecast::seed_1_default_cores_every_eighth` | Go's three cases: seed 1 with 2 cores, seed 7 with 2 cores and a crash joint, and seed 1 with 16 cores checking every eighth intent. |
| `tuner/forecast_sim_test.go` `BenchmarkDashboardProjectionForecast` | none | No counterpart. This is a performance probe, not a behavior test: it times `Replay`, the dashboard queries and `Forecast` over the 16-core seed-1 journal. The Rust crates have no benchmark harness yet: no `[[bench]]` target and no criterion. Stage 3's closing measurements, under "Forecast cost, against Go's", measured the full `replay` fold and `forecast` against Go's figures, on a different session. They did not time the dashboard views. |

Some Rust tests have no Go counterpart. They pin behavior Go got wrong or did not have: `log::a_log_that_fails_during_a_crashed_boot_fails_the_simulation_instead_of_capping_it`, `log::an_observer_sees_every_append_after_the_log_fails`, `log::an_observer_sees_the_journal_in_order_across_boots` and its log, failing-log and on-disk variants, `log::a_stopped_observer_is_not_called_again_and_the_run_is_cancelled_once`, `simulate::tests::a_boot_that_ends_without_a_crash_is_always_checked`; the `persistence::` tests that pin when a state directory on disk gets `state.json`: a warm simulation gets it only when it stops (`a_warm_simulation_on_disk_gets_state_json_only_when_it_stops`), a cold one at every crashed boot (`a_cold_simulation_on_disk_gets_state_json_at_every_crashed_boot`), a capped one keeps the crashed run's and a signalled one publishes it at its stop, never during a run; the `split::` tests, which cover a capped simulation continuing in another process from `machine.json`, in memory too, and a snapshot that cannot be read or removed; and the `boundary::` tests, which cover rejected persistence options, a host failure at startup, the repaired-tail log line, `dump_to`, and reading the live journal back.

## Stage 4: simulated reboot keeps the journal (#873, 2026-10-12)

Go resumed a simulated reboot from the crashed boot's memory: no journal re-read, and `state.json` left pending after a crash until the invocation ended (`TestCrashLeavesDeferredStateUnwrittenInWarm`). Rust's `simulate` read and parsed all of `events.jsonl` at every boot, in memory and on disk, and published `state.json` at every crash, which the next boot then read back to compare.

A warm reboot now carries state forward:

- `Journal::reboot` keeps the open journal across a simulated reboot: it writes the buffer through, sets the new boot and a new `JournalId`, so no receipt of the crashed boot verifies, clears `repaired`, and reads nothing. A poisoned journal is not rebooted; the simulation closes it and takes the `lock` and `prepare` path.
- `togi_session::prepare_kept` makes `prepare`'s decisions from the open journal's events. A journal `prepare` would archive before opening (at the reset boundary, a build that only differs, a build this build refuses) is closed and handed to `prepare`, so the directory work is the same code. A table test pins `prepare_kept` against `prepare` on the same directory for an empty journal, a plain resume, a build archive, a context change, a settled and an unsettled carry and a reset boundary.
- A run that adopts a capsule skips `rebuild_state`: the boot before it left `state.json` pending on purpose. `ResumeCapsule::covers` is the adoption test, and `ResumeCapsule::publish` writes into a `StateDir`.
- With `Replay::Warm` a crash publishes nothing; the last boot's capsule is published when a run stops or `max_boots` caps the simulation, when a warm boot cannot keep the journal (its flush at the reboot failed, or the capsule does not cover it) and on any error exit after a warm crash, as Go's deferred `TakePending` did. `Replay::Cold` publishes at every crash, since nothing adopts there.

`events.jsonl` and the final `state.json` stay byte-identical to the cold path (`warm_cold` and `persistence` tests, unchanged). New tests: `reboot::a_warm_simulation_reads_the_journal_once_however_many_boots_it_runs` counts reads of `events.jsonl` through `MemFs::reads`, `reboot::a_warm_simulation_publishes_state_json_once_at_its_stop_or_its_cap` counts publications, with cold counterparts, and `togi-journal` and `togi-session` pin `reboot`, `prepare_kept` and the adoption.

Measured with release `togi-tool-sim --seed 102 --machine tools/bench/machines/flat-hazard.json --cycles 20 --state-dir D` (87,846 events, a 36.7 MB journal, about 290 crashes), on a shared host, three runs each:

| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| wall time | 36.8 s, 19.2 s, 18.3 s | 1.5 s, 1.5 s, 1.4 s |
| `read` calls (`strace -f -c`) | 1,767 | 9 |
| `rename` calls | 294 | 1 |
| `write` calls | 9,147 | 8,854 |

`events.jsonl` and `state.json` of the two builds compare byte-identical. The per-`smu.intent` flush stays, a measured deviation from Go, which flushes its journal buffer only on close, read-back or a full buffer: Rust issues a `write` at every intent, so this session makes 8,854 `write` calls, but they cost 3 ms of syscall time in a 3 s run, so dropping the flush would not show.

CI suite, `rust-sim-suite-0` to `rust-sim-suite-3`: release `togi-tool-sim suite --suite tools/bench/suite.json --shards 4 --shard I --jobs 32`, the command `flake.nix` runs, timed with GNU `time` at the shard level, three runs of each shard on a 32-thread shared host under varying load (load average 12 to 29), the base at 34c29cd4 and the head at 28f3d96d, each built apart:

| shard | wall before, three runs | wall after | CPU (user + sys) before | CPU after |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 14.2, 18.5, 18.7 s | 3.7, 3.9, 3.6 s | 224, 230, 241 s | 57, 59, 56 s |
| 1 | 14.4, 19.2, 15.3 s | 3.7, 4.0, 3.9 s | 220, 250, 229 s | 56, 55, 55 s |
| 2 | 16.8, 19.0, 15.4 s | 3.9, 4.3, 4.3 s | 225, 244, 225 s | 57, 56, 57 s |
| 3 | 13.6, 19.9, 16.2 s | 3.6, 3.8, 4.1 s | 213, 240, 220 s | 55, 54, 60 s |

The slowest shard takes 16.8, 19.9 and 18.7 s before and 3.9, 4.3 and 4.3 s after; the four shards together take 883, 964 and 914 CPU seconds before and 225, 224 and 228 after. Each shard has 55 sessions and every one ends `clean`, on both builds.

The slowest sessions, as `suite` reports each one's time (median of the three runs):

| session | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| `target-r7-request-gap/holdout-105` | 14.5 s | 2.6 s |
| `target-r7-request-gap/holdout-104` | 14.5 s | 2.3 s |
| `target-r7-request-gap/dev-5` | 14.0 s | 3.1 s |
| `target-r7-request-gap/holdout-107` | 13.9 s | 3.5 s |
| `target-r7-request-gap/holdout-112` | 13.8 s | 2.1 s |
| `flat-hazard/dev-3` | 9.5 s | 3.5 s |

Before, the six slowest sessions were all `target-r7-request-gap`; after, the slowest is `flat-hazard/dev-3` at 3.5 s, then `target-r7-request-gap/holdout-107` at 3.5 s. A session's own time includes contention with the others of its shard, so the figures compare builds, not absolute costs. The sum over the 220 sessions of the median times falls from 965 s to 240 s.

The same sessions alone, one at a time, with nothing else of the suite running: the release `togi-tool-sim suite --suite S --shards 1 --shard 0 --out D --jobs 1`, where `S` is a suite file of just that session (its scenario's name, machine and `replay` from `tools/bench/suite.json`, and its one seed). `suite` has no session filter, and this runs the suite's own `execute` path: in-memory state directory, `Replay::Warm`, the resume state checked after every crash, one cycle. Timed with GNU `time -f '%e %U %S'`, three runs each on the shared host, base 34c29cd4 and head 28f3d96d; every run ended `clean`. CPU is user plus sys, wall is the process's elapsed time, three runs listed in order, median in bold:

| session | wall before | wall after | CPU before | CPU after |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `target-r7-request-gap/holdout-105` | 6.24, 6.41, 6.21 s (**6.24**) | 1.70, 1.69, 1.70 s (**1.70**) | 6.19, 6.37, 6.14 s (**6.19**) | 1.68, 1.66, 1.68 s (**1.68**) |
| `target-r7-request-gap/holdout-104` | 5.54, 5.50, 5.56 s (**5.54**) | 1.47, 1.48, 1.46 s (**1.47**) | 5.50, 5.46, 5.46 s (**5.46**) | 1.46, 1.46, 1.45 s (**1.46**) |
| `target-r7-request-gap/dev-5` | 8.86, 8.30, 8.34 s (**8.34**) | 2.17, 2.18, 2.17 s (**2.17**) | 8.78, 8.25, 8.31 s (**8.31**) | 2.16, 2.17, 2.15 s (**2.16**) |
| `target-r7-request-gap/holdout-107` | 9.09, 9.00, 9.00 s (**9.00**) | 2.35, 2.52, 2.39 s (**2.39**) | 9.05, 8.95, 8.95 s (**8.95**) | 2.33, 2.49, 2.37 s (**2.37**) |
| `target-r7-request-gap/holdout-112` | 5.54, 5.61, 5.57 s (**5.57**) | 1.48, 1.48, 1.47 s (**1.48**) | 5.52, 5.57, 5.54 s (**5.54**) | 1.47, 1.48, 1.47 s (**1.47**) |
| `flat-hazard/dev-3` | 5.33, 5.38, 5.93 s (**5.38**) | 2.46, 2.50, 2.92 s (**2.50**) | 5.30, 5.35, 5.90 s (**5.35**) | 2.44, 2.48, 2.90 s (**2.48**) |

Alone, a session is CPU-bound (user plus sys is within 0.1 s of wall in every run), and each falls 2.2 to 3.9 times in CPU and in wall. After, `flat-hazard/dev-3` (2.50 s) and `target-r7-request-gap/holdout-107` (2.39 s) are the slowest alone too, as in the shards; before, `holdout-107` and `dev-5` (9.00 and 8.34 s) lead alone, and `flat-hazard/dev-3` is the cheapest of the six (5.38 s), so its 9.5 s in a shard was mostly contention. The in-shard times above are inflated by load; the alone times are the sessions' cost.

Byte identity across the suite: all 220 sessions of the four shards ran again on each build with their in-memory state directories dumped to disk, and every session's `events.jsonl`, `state.json` and `lock` compare byte-identical between the builds (`cmp` on each file, `diff -r` on the trees: 0 of 220 sessions differ), as do the four shard manifests.

Tests, `cargo nextest run -p togi-simrun -p togi-session -p togi-journal`, on the same shared host: 808 tests before, 822 after, all passing; the run takes 2.7 s and 3.2 s before and 2.8 s and 2.1 s after. A first run recompiled the three crates in 14.3 s before and 13.5 s after, with their dependencies already built. The pull request changes 25 files, adding 708 lines and removing 169 (`git diff --shortstat 34c29cd4 28f3d96d`).

Regressions: none found. The `read` and `rename` counts fall, the `write` count falls by 3 %, and the suite and the tests take no longer.

Review findings, all fixed: a warm crash left the state pending when the simulation then ended on an error, which Go's deferred `TakePending` wrote, so every error exit now publishes it; the failed flush at a reboot, which poisons the journal, had no test (`a_reboot_whose_sync_fails_poisons_the_journal_and_keeps_its_boot` pins it); `TestSetBootPreservesEarlierBootEvidence` had no row in the Go-test mapping; and `simulating.md` contradicted itself and the code on when a warm crash publishes `state.json`.

Not measured: peak memory of the suite (the journal is now held open and its events parsed once, so the resident set may differ), a run on macOS, the `togi run` path (unchanged, so not timed), and compile times of a clean build.

## Stage 4: crash-sweep test time (#842, 2026-10-12)

The two `togi-session` crash sweeps hit nextest's 120 s limit on GitHub runners ([#842](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/842)). `killing_the_run_at_every_durable_event_resumes_to_the_same_final_state` kills a run at each of the 809 durable events of a one-cycle session, and `a_crash_at_every_trial_event_leaves_a_resumable_journal` crashes one at each of 664 trial events. Each point drives a whole session again, about 85 ms in the dev profile, so the cost is quadratic in the journal length. A perf profile of the sweep put about 32 % of the time in `Journal::commit` decoding each written line again, 22 % in `StateDir::open_at` parsing the journal at every boot, 20 % in the tuner fold and 12 % in encoding: nearly all of it unoptimized serde_json.

- **Change.** Dependencies, `togi-journal` and `togi-tuner` build at `opt-level = 1` in the dev profile; debug assertions and overflow checks stay on. [#859](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/859) later moved the whole workspace to `opt-level = 1` in dev. The sweeps count durable lines without decoding them and fold each swept journal once. Every durable event is still a kill point and every trial event a crash point.
- **Measurements.** 4 CPUs (`taskset -c 0-3`), `cargo nextest run -p togi-session --profile ci`, two runs each, on a shared host:

  | | before | test changes | test changes + opt-level 1 |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `killing_the_run_at_every_durable_event_…` | 27.3 s, 27.9 s | 26.0 s, 24.6 s | 9.3 s, 9.3 s |
  | `a_crash_at_every_trial_event_…` | 25.8 s, 25.7 s | 25.5 s, 25.3 s | 8.6 s, 8.4 s |
  | whole `togi-session` suite | 30.2 s, 30.3 s | 28.3 s, 27.2 s | 10.1 s, 10.0 s |

  An earlier run of the base under heavier host load took 48.1 s and 38.8 s.
- **Cost.** An incremental dev rebuild after a one-line change (`cargo build --tests -p <crate>`, hyperfine, 6 runs, 1 warmup) goes from 2.26 ± 0.03 s to 3.81 ± 0.06 s for `togi-journal`, and from 4.62 ± 0.04 s to 8.44 ± 0.99 s for `togi-tuner`.
- **Coverage.** `just cover-rs` still reports changed lines correctly at opt-level 1. A throwaway function was added to each of `togi-journal` and `togi-tuner`, with a test that took one branch, and then dropped. For both crates the tool reported exactly the two lines of the untaken branch and none of the lines the test reached.
- **Not measured.** The times on GitHub runners themselves, which the pull request's checks show.

## Stage 7: `tools/modelcheck` (#816, 2026-10-12)

The `togi-tool-modelcheck` crate ports `tools/modelcheck` ([#816](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/816)): 612 lines of library and 698 of tests, against Go's 305 and 301. No Go code is removed or changed. It is a library for `tools/fit` and `tools/bench`.

- **Two ways in, one verdict.** `check(machine_path, &definition, &mut Extracts)` and `check_records(machine, extract, &Model, &records)` return a `Check` for a report (`report` prints it; `Check` and `Group` serialize). `Checker::new(&Model, &records)` groups the evidence once; `Checker::accepts(&Model)` answers a search's candidate without interval work, and `Checker::groups` / `Checker::check` fill in each group's mean probability, interval and flag. A test pins that `accepts` and a report's `ok` agree, and a property pins that a count is inside the interval exactly when `p` is inside the group's precomputed bounds.
- **The predictor is `togi_sim::Model`.** Go's `Predictor` interface, which a `Machine` and a `Predictor` both satisfied, is gone: the model already owns `failure_probability`. A model of another shape than the checker's is a `ShapeError`, never a wrong verdict.
- **Typed evidence.** A group is keyed by BIOS context, `TrialClass` and a `TargetOffset` depth, not by a JSON string; the `Kind` field is gone because only trials are grouped, and a trial that is neither pass nor failure, or a kind that is neither trial nor idle, cannot be written in a `TrialFact`. Groups come out ordered by context, workload, loaded cores, duration and depth. Facts are validated against the model: one offset per core, a loaded core that exists, a nonzero duration, each refused as `Error::InvalidFact` naming the session and sequence.
- **Log-binomial coefficients.** std has no `lgamma`, so `ln C(n, k)` comes from a Neumaier-compensated table of `ln(i!)`; `Binomial(10000, 0.5)` still gives `[4871, 5129]`.
- **Own formats.** A report line lists loaded cores as `0,1` and prints `mean_p` as `{:.6e}`; `Check` serializes `interval` as `{low, high}`, the class as `regime`, `workload`, `cores`, `duration_s` and the status as `ok` or `flagged`. Go's `testdata` fixtures (a Go-written extract with a workload outside the catalog) are replaced by fixtures the tests write with `togi_tool_trialfacts::write`.
- **Go bugs fixed.** None found. The reset check, group threshold, bounds bisection and interval rule are as in Go; `docs/benchmarking.md` already describes them, so no spec changes.
- **Tests.** Every Go test maps to a Rust test or a stated reason (mapping in the pull request). `go test -count=1 ./tools/modelcheck` took 0.006 s (11 tests); `cargo nextest run -p togi-tool-modelcheck` ran 26 tests in 0.04 s on a built tree.
- **Not measured.** Compile times against Go, and agent cost. Not run on a Mac.
- **Review findings fixed.** Groups were keyed by class and depth only, so a machine that declares no BIOS context pooled trials recorded under different contexts into one group, labelled by whichever fact came first; the context is now part of the key, as in `docs/benchmarking.md`. A missing or corrupt facts extract reported "load replay oracle: ..." although no replay is involved; `Error::ReadExtract` now names the extract read and keeps the `ReadError` as its source.

## Stage 7: `tools/forecast` (#817, 2026-10-12)

The `togi-tool-forecast` crate ports `tools/forecast` ([#817](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/817)): the library `tools/bench --forecast` (#823) writes forecasts with and `tools/stats --forecast` (#820) scores them with. It is the pure part: the anchor, a run's outcome after it, the summary of an ensemble, the committed record and the score of the real run. It starts no simulation: the runs, their seeds and their order belong to the caller, and every function is a pure function of its inputs, so the record does not depend on how the runs were scheduled. It needs `togi-journal`, `togi-machine`, `togi-sim` (the reboot time the bias note states) and `togi-tuner`, and not `togi-session`: this build's ruleset is `togi_tuner::RULESET`.

- **API.** `Anchor::of(dir)` and `Session::anchor_at`, `read_dir(dir, from)` (read-only, no lock), `after(sessions, anchor) -> Outcome`, `Record::new(...)`, `Record::write` and `Record::read`, `Record::score(sessions) -> Score`, `metrics` and `summarize`. Statuses, metric kinds, placements and flags are enums; a profile is `PerCore<ObservedOffset>`, so a run's cores are named by id and a forecast and a run over different cores are refused by id, not by count.
- **Design calls.** The anchor hash covers the journal lines as the file holds them, kept as one running SHA-256 prefix per event, so a build that later encodes events differently still scores an old forecast; a session built in memory hashes the events' own encodings, which are the journal's bytes (a test reads a real simulated journal back to prove it). The replay rule is `Build::verdict(Operation::Replay)` on the session's build; a session keeps its `Refusal`, so `NotReplayable` names the dimension (schema or ruleset) and both values. The record is the Go field set with `evidence` added, `outcome` nested in each run and `profile` and `cores` objects of core to value; it is the Rust record, which `Record::read` holds to that layout and so refuses a Go-written forecast (the Rust build reads nothing the Go tools wrote). The counting rules run against a small private `Replay` seam, so they are pinned without a journal the tuner would accept; the real tuner runs in the whole-session tests, one of which pins its checking profile against the journal's last `profile.change`.
- **Go bugs fixed.** A fold error of the journal state or the tuner was dropped, so a damaged session scored from a half-folded profile; it is an error naming the session (`after::a_journal_that_does_not_follow_itself_is_a_fold_error`, `a_journal_the_tuner_cannot_follow_is_a_fold_error`). Scoring compared only the number of cores, so a forecast and a run over different core sets of one size scored against each other's columns; it compares the sets (`score::a_run_over_other_cores_than_the_forecast_is_refused_though_their_count_matches`). The percentile of a metric without values was `NaN`; it is `None` (`summary::without_a_concluded_run_hours_have_no_range`). A forecast of one evidence epoch scored against a run of another without a flag; it carries `Flag::EvidenceEpoch` (`score::a_dirty_forecast_and_another_ruleset_or_evidence_epoch_are_flagged`). An unknown run status was a late error in `Summarize`; it cannot be decoded (`record::a_record_with_an_unknown_status_is_refused_on_reading`). Go ignored unknown keys in a record, so a misspelled `hours` read as no concluded runs; the Rust record refuses them (`record::an_unknown_key_is_refused_in_every_object_of_a_record`). Go's decoder already rejected an array for a struct, which serde's derive would accept by position, so every object of the Rust record is object-only (`record::a_correctly_sized_array_is_refused_for_every_object_of_a_record`).
- **Not ported.** Go read a session the strict reader refused through a lenient history reader and called it not replayable; the Rust build has one reader, so a journal that does not decode is an error naming the file (`files::a_journal_that_fails_the_readers_checks_is_an_error_naming_the_file`), while an archive older than the anchor's session is skipped unread. No Go journal is read.
- **Tests.** 75 tests run in about 6.2 s of test time under `cargo nextest run -p togi-tool-forecast`, against 0.18 s for `go test -count=1 ./tools/forecast`. Almost all of it is the endless-run test (`simulated::an_endless_run_scores_as_the_cycles_one_run_though_it_crashes_after_concluding`, about 6.1 s), the port of Go's `TestAfterScoresAnEndlessRunAsACyclesOneRun` on `togi_simrun::simulate`: a 16-core history simulated to its first `crash.detected`, then two copies resumed on a near-limit-failing machine with another seed, one with `--cycles 1` and one without a limit that checks one more cycle and crashes in it; the second scores exactly as the first. The two-core cycles-2 test (0.2 s) stays: it pins the real tuner's checking profile against the journal's last `profile.change` and the session baseline. About 1,230 lines of library and 2,310 of tests, against Go's 615 and 608. No Go code is removed or changed.
- **Not measured.** Compile times against Go, agent cost, and the run on a Mac.

## Stage 6: `togi-watch` model, history and words (#833, 2026-10-12)

The `togi-watch` crate begins with the model of the `togi watch` dashboard ([#833](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/833), part of [#641](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/641)): `model`, `history`, `words` and the `screen` seam the loop and the frames share, with no terminal output. It ports `model.go`, `snapshot.go` and `history.go` and the word tables of [#565](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/565): 3,707 lines of library and 4,921 of tests against Go's 1,962 for those three files and 1,079 for their tests (`snapshot_test.go`, `history_test.go`, `accessors_test.go`), with the Go package unchanged. 119 tests.

- **Shape.** `Model::fold(&Event)` takes each event once, in the journal's, the tuner's and then the watch's own state; `snapshot()` is a `Copy` struct of borrowed views, built once per change from the tuner's `project()` and `forecast()` and cached until the next fold. Go projected the whole journal for every frame. A fold the journal or the tuner refuses makes the model a problem state that keeps the error, so a frame has one thing to say. `words(kind)` is an exhaustive match over `EventKind` that says for each kind whether the log lists it and how its history line, log tag, compact outcome and detailed phrase read, so a new event kind does not compile until it is worded; a test pins the silent kinds. The frames, the narration and the loop read the model and `words`; none of them reads the journal.
- **Native, not Go.** The model follows the native journal: no record-only trials, so no `record` tag and no `signal` or `tail` entry fields; profiles are keyed by core id, so the position-versus-id handling of sparse core ids is gone; `Regime`, `Signal` and `WorkloadId` are closed enums, so the text fallbacks for unknown values are gone; a core's solo limit is the tuner's, not a second map kept by the watch, so a core that exited without a pass has none, and its search exit has no solo limit line or phrase either. A core's move with no history line of its own is tagged in the log by the phase it enters (`step`, `room` or `limit`), so a core entering its search at the start of a session is no longer tagged `limit`, and its phrase says it starts its search, not that it starts over. With record-only trials gone, no crash line carries a merge key, so Go's refold of a recovered record-only crash line into the line before it is not ported. A `hold` decision, which only the native tuner makes, has the tag `hold` and a history line. History facts stay raw: the frames escape, as the model does not know the terminal.
- **Go bugs fixed.** The view of a trial a crash left unrecovered was built with no events, so its start and partial flag were never set and the recovery frame could not tell a part from a full load; it is built as the running trial's view is.
- **Defects found before review.** Coverage found that the cycle's steps were never recorded from `checking.cycle`, so a step line never named its regime; the steps are folded now and a test pins the line.
- **Review findings fixed.** Round 1 found one P2 code defect: while a rerun ran, the step checking resumes at was read only from the forecast between trials, which the tuner leaves empty while a trial is open, so the step fell back to the plan's current step and did not skip the steps the tuner records only chain endings for; it is now read from the pass branch while a trial runs, as Go read it from its if-pass branch, and a test drives reruns and compares the step named with the next cycle trial's. The other findings were tests that did not pin what they named (the unrecovered crash's start and partial load, a later boot ending an earlier recovery, the idle hold released by a later boot, refolding after a snapshot, a tuner refusal, the session activity, the roles of a parked group's members, decision tones and log tags), an unused `signal` entry field, `_` arms over domain enums, a search exit with no pass worded as a solo limit, and a core's first move into its search, which the log tagged `limit` and the phrase called a start over: it is now tagged `step`, and its phrase says the core starts its search.
- **Tests.** The Go tests of `snapshot_test.go` and `history_test.go` map as the pull request lists. The tests of `story_test.go`, `narration_test.go`, `cycle_resume_test.go` and `r7_status_test.go` that assert a model fact (the idle hold, the past-end clock, the unrecovered crash, the resume step, the R7 top requesters) are ported here; those that assert wording wait for the narration ([#835](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/835)). At the pull request head, `just cover-rs` against the base reports one changed line no test reaches, `crates/togi-watch/src/describe.rs:84`: the `_ => None` arm of the contextual history dispatch, which no kind the table marks contextual reaches.
- **Spec.** `docs/spec/runtime.md` is unchanged: the Dashboard section says what the dashboard shows, and no behaviour it describes changed.
- **Not measured.** Compile times, the comparison with `go test ./internal/watch`, agent cost, and the cost of a snapshot on a long recorded session.

## Stage 6: `togi-watchtest` (#834, 2026-10-12)

`togi-watchtest` is a dev-dependency-only crate that builds the simulated session journals the watch tests read: the dashboard's frame tests, replay and stage 7's status tests. It replaces Go's `frames_test.go` helpers and the gzip recordings in `internal/watch/watchtest`, which Rust never reads.

- `Scenario` names the 18 watch golden scenarios that have a journal. `Scenario::events()` cuts one from a `Base` simulation. Each of the 9 bases is simulated once per process (`OnceLock`); `Base::simulate()` runs one afresh and `Scenario::events_from` cuts from it.
- `simulate(machine, config, until, stop)` runs `togi-simrun` in memory. Every journal names `build()`: version `0.0.0`, no revision, and the tuner's current ruleset and evidence epoch, so a release's version bump changes no frame. `until` is optional, and without it no observer runs.
- `cut_at`, `cut_when`, `cut_trial`, `cut_passed_trial`, `cut_passed_checking_trial`, `hunt_with_unconfirmed_profile` and `before_crash_detected` are Go's cut helpers, returning `Result` in place of failing the test.
- `install(dir, scenario)` writes `events.jsonl` through `togi-journal`'s writer, one open journal per boot, and returns the session's `Build`. A directory that already holds a journal is refused: the writer would append the scenario after it.
- `cycle_completed_by_rerun()` is Go's fixture of the same name, built by answering the real tuner's `next()` with no simulator.

Where the Rust simulator differs from Go's, a scenario is cut by the state its name claims, not by Go's event rule. `between` cuts after the first passed checking cycle trial: the first passed checking trial of the default session is a rerun. `unconfirmed` uses seed 43: Go's seed 30 never leaves a BIOS offset unconfirmed in the Rust simulator, and a scan of seeds 1 to 60 found one in seed 43 only.

The later-hunt base runs once, through three clean cycles. Go simulated six cycles twice: once to find the first checking trial of cycle 4, then again with the burst of crashes scripted from that trial on. Trials before the burst run alike either way, so the burst's start is pinned (trial 1887) and the burst's own journal is checked to show it; three cycles reach the same hunt, and the `hunt-bios` cut is byte-identical to the six-cycle one.

`deadend` is a journal the runner writes, not Go's synthetic event. Go appended a `no_evidence` dead end a minute after an R7 checking trial started, with no cause, beside a trial still running. The Rust runner checks for a dead end only after a trial ends, and every `no_evidence` dead end cites its evidence. The `DeadEnd` base runs the default machine and makes its mprime backend go missing once the first R7 checking cycle trial has started: that trial ends, the next mprime trial cannot start, and the session records the dead end citing the `backend_missing` end of the trial that could not start.

- **Measured.** Against `c9ba3004`: 1198 lines of library (with the manifest) and 803 of tests, none removed, next to Go's 441 test-helper lines (`frames_test.go` lines 27-336, `watchtest/fixture.go`, and the two fixtures of `cycle_resume_test.go`), which built journals and pinned nothing. On a 32-thread host at load 17 to 21, in the debug profile after #845 (dependencies, `togi-journal` and `togi-tuner` at `opt-level = 1`): the base simulations take 0.19 s (dead end) to 0.95 s (later hunt); the suite takes 2.5 to 5.2 s with nextest's parallelism, and the slowest test, the later-hunt determinism test, 2.2 s run alone and 5.1 s under `cargo llvm-cov` (`just cover-rs`). Before #845 the suite took 8.9 s and its slowest test 8.5 s (11.7 s under `cargo llvm-cov`). Review round 1 found 5 issues, all fixed: the crashed-cycle base's property unpinned, Go's impossible dead-end journal, `install` appending over an existing journal, these missing measurements, and a `cut_when` test that could not tell the state before the fold from the state after it.
- **Not measured.** Compile times against Go, and agent cost.

## Stage 7: `tools/fit` (#818, 2026-10-12)

The `togi-tool-fit` crate ports `tools/fit` ([#818](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/818)): a command crate, a library of 2,708 lines and a clap `main.rs` of 84, against Go's 1,934 lines of library and command; 2,939 lines of tests (61 tests) against Go's 1,973 (37 tests). No Go code is removed or changed; the recipes `fit-rs`, `fit-shared-voltage-rs` and `forward-rs` sit beside `fit`, `fit-shared-voltage` and `forward`.

- **Determinism across threads.** Every search step builds a vector of candidates and scores them with `par_iter().map().collect()`, so each result has its own index; the winner is then picked by a sequential fold with a strict `< score - 1e-9` test, in candidate order. A score sums its observations in index order on one thread, and no float is summed across threads. The joint candidates, the prefix fits of the forward check and the ensemble's members use the same index-slot pattern, and the first error is taken in index order; every fit runs to completion after a failure, so the error reported does not depend on which thread failed first. `--jobs` builds a pool of that size around the whole run (Go's `--jobs` bounded only the whole fits). `tests/fit/determinism.rs` runs one ensemble with its forward-chained check, and the shared-voltage anchor, on explicit pools of 1, 2 and all CPUs and compares the machine files, the reports and the forward rows; replacing the in-order observation sum with a rayon `sum` fails both.
- **Design calls.**
  - The machine being fitted is a `MachineDefinition`, rebuilt into a `Model` for each candidate; a candidate the model refuses (an overflowing rate) has infinite loss, so a search never keeps it, and the final machine is rebuilt with the error returned.
  - `Likelihood` is one struct with the optional shared-voltage terms (the threshold prior and the named-core failure share) instead of Go's embedded `voltageLikelihood`. Here every step minimizes the same objective, which fixes a Go bug (below). The anchor's likelihood on the committed extract is unchanged to 17 digits.
  - The bootstrap draws come from `StdRng` seeded `seed + n`, so refits 1 to 8 differ from the committed files; machine files are the simulator crate's own encoding.
  - Facts are typed: a trial's workload is in the catalog, offsets are in range and a failure always names a signal, so Go's checks for those and the "failure without a signal is skipped" rule are gone; a BIOS context with an empty field is kept as is, which drops Go's "every field set" requirement of the anchor.
  - `togi-tool-modelcheck` supplies the checker. Report lines, the elapsed times (printed by `main.rs` after the report) and the notes follow Rust's formats; `docs/benchmarking.md` has a section on the differences.
- **Go bugs fixed.** The anchor's shared steps (hazard shape, flat, idle, limit-rate shift) called Go's embedded raw `score`, without the threshold prior and the named-core term, while the voltage steps, the constrained rate search and the convergence test (`previous - score < 1e-5`) used the full objective. A shared step could therefore lower the raw loss while raising the measured objective, and the sweep then stopped early. Every step now minimizes the objective the sweep measures; `the_shared_steps_minimize_the_objective_the_anchor_sweep_converges_on` fails if a shared step reverts to the raw one. The committed extract's anchor likelihood is unchanged.
- **Measured.** On the committed extract at this head, the pooled line of `togi-tool-fit`'s forward-chained check prints the Go tool's to the digit (`log_loss/trial fit=0.6089`, `flagged/eligible=13/23`, `failures_p<0.01=88`); `target-fit-0` reaches negative log likelihood 209.517088 and the anchor 283.79098647702926, both the committed values. Wall time: `just fit` 4.5 s in Go against 4.7 s in Rust and `--forward-only` 2.9 s against 2.1 s, on one 16-core host each, one run each. `go test -count=1 ./tools/fit` took 0.76 s (37 tests); `cargo nextest run -p togi-tool-fit` ran 61 tests in 13.0 s on a built debug tree on a busy host, the longest the anchor's pool-size determinism test at 13.0 s and the ensemble's at 6.3 s.
- **Review findings fixed.** Round 1 found no code defect; it found that the shared objective above was a Go bug fix without a test, and test gaps: the anchor was never run on more than one pool size, the constrained rate search's rate-raising branch was never reached, the restarted constrained refits were not checked to be unflagged with their resample's loss, and an anchor with an empty BIOS field was not checked to replay. Each gap now has a test that fails on the broken behavior. It also found that this entry's test times were not from one run (a 2.1 s test in a 2.0 s run); they are re-measured above.
- **Not measured.** Compile times against Go, agent cost, and a Mac: not run on a Mac.

## Stage 5: close (#768, 2026-10-12)

Stage 5 ([#640](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/640)) closes with its nine crates on `main` at `113b73ee`: `togi-hostlock`, `togi-command`, `togi-smu`, `togi-backend`, `togi-mprime`, `togi-ycruncher`, `togi-detect`, `togi-trial` and `togi-hardware`. This pull request adds the recipe for the Rust hardware tests and the stage's measurements. No Go is removed or changed.

- **Stage 4 suite check.** `just sim-suite-rs --jobs 8` on `main` at `97eba4cc` (with this pull request's recipe and log entry, which change no code): all 220 suite sessions ended `clean` and `togi-tool-sim check --shards 1` passed, in 128 s of wall time including the release build, on the 32-thread development host at a load of 32 to 36. CI's `rust-sim-suite-0` to `-3` run the same suite on every pull request.
- **The recipe.** `just hardware-rs` sits beside `just hardware`. It enters the dev shell itself, since `sudo` drops it, and runs `cargo test --locked --no-fail-fast --features hardware --test hardware` for `togi-smu`, `togi-detect`, `togi-trial` and `togi-hardware` with `RUST_TEST_THREADS=1`: cargo runs the binaries one after another, all of them even after one fails, and each runs one test at a time, and each test that needs the host takes the host lock. Trial's `cached_errors` binary needs an ordinary account (`BackendIdentity` refuses uid 0), so the recipe adds it only when it does not run as root; such a run fails the tests that need root. As root it builds in a target directory under root's home, so it leaves no root-owned files in the checkout's `target/`. The recipe refuses outside Linux. `just hardware-rs --no-run` builds the binaries and starts none. The owner runs it as root on the target machine at the cutover ([#643](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/643)); the VM check of `togi-trial` ran its tests under real systemd, and the SMU tests have run nowhere.
- **Hardware-feature tests built.** `just hardware-rs --no-run` and `cargo test --locked -p togi-trial -p togi-hardware --features hardware --no-run` each built every binary. `--list` shows 2 tests in `togi-smu`, 1 in `togi-detect`, 12 in the `togi-trial` `hardware` binary, 2 in its `cached_errors` and 1 in `togi-hardware`. None ran.
- **Test counts and times.** One `cargo nextest run --locked -p <crate>` per crate on Linux, as an ordinary user, on a warm build with other jobs on the host, one run each; Go is `go test -count=1` of the matching package, all in one invocation (its packages run in parallel). Go's `internal/backend` has no test files, and `togi-command` has no Go package.

| Crate | Rust tests | Rust time [s] | Go package | Go time [s] |
|:---|---:|---:|:---|---:|
| `togi-hostlock` | 20 | 0.005 | `internal/hostlock` | 0.002 |
| `togi-command` | 38 | 6.252 | none | none |
| `togi-smu` | 85 | 0.222 | `internal/smu` | 0.057 |
| `togi-backend` | 16 | 0.004 | `internal/backend` | no tests |
| `togi-mprime` | 20 | 0.208 | `internal/backend/mprime` | 0.004 |
| `togi-ycruncher` | 36 | 0.459 | `internal/backend/ycruncher` | 0.004 |
| `togi-detect` | 85 | 1.021 | `internal/detect` | 0.003 |
| `togi-trial` | 440 | 8.623 | `internal/trial` | 0.125 |
| `togi-hardware` | 101 | 0.313 | `internal/hardware` | 0.005 |
| Total | 841 | 17.107 | | 0.200 |

The counts, times and sizes are at `113b73ee`. Since then only [#807](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/807) changed a stage 5 crate: `togi-trial` gained 136 test lines and kept 440 tests, which ran in 8.8 s on `97eba4cc` at a load of 36.

- **Size.** Library and test lines of each crate, counted as the `togi-trial` entry counts: each source file is split at its `#[cfg(test)]` module, matching braces, and the module, files of modules declared under `#[cfg(test)]` (such as `togi-trial`'s `src/os/fake.rs`), files named `tests.rs` or `testing.rs`, `src/tests/` and `tests/` are tests. Go's are the `.go` and `_test.go` files of the package.

| Crate | Rust library | Rust tests | Go library | Go tests |
|:---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `togi-hostlock` | 351 | 598 | 148 | 400 |
| `togi-command` | 553 | 1,038 | 0 | 0 |
| `togi-smu` | 2,043 | 3,209 | 795 | 1,284 |
| `togi-backend` | 246 | 250 | 60 | 130 |
| `togi-mprime` | 186 | 399 | 84 | 228 |
| `togi-ycruncher` | 263 | 722 | 136 | 324 |
| `togi-detect` | 1,833 | 2,976 | 736 | 1,259 |
| `togi-trial` | 7,469 | 16,285 | 2,200 | 6,195 |
| `togi-hardware` | 1,548 | 2,299 | 467 | 877 |
| Total | 14,492 | 27,776 | 4,626 | 10,697 |

- **Reading them.** The nine crates hold 3.1 times Go's library lines and 2.6 times its test lines, counting `togi-command`, which has no Go package. Their 841 tests run in 17.1 s, 6.3 s of it in `togi-command` (its entry says why) and 8.6 s in `togi-trial`; Go's seven packages take 0.2 s together, the longest, `internal/trial`, 0.125 s. The comparison is of suites, not of the code under test: the Rust tests start real processes that the Go unit tests do not, and the counts and times were not normalised for that.
- **Not measured.** Compile times against Go, agent cost, changed-line coverage of the stage as a whole, and any run of the recipe, since it was only built. Nothing ran on real hardware and the suite was not run on a Mac. The NixOS VM check of the trial scope was not rerun here; CI runs it as `vm-trial-scope-rs` ([#809](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/809)).

## Stage 7: `tools/stats` (#820, 2026-10-12)

The `togi-tool-stats` crate ports `tools/stats` ([#820](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/820)): `togi-tool-stats [--state-dir DIR | --journal FILE] [--since TIME]` prints the review report of a current or archived journal, and `--forecast FILE` scores the run in `--state-dir` against a forecast instead. The library is `Journal::read` (or `Journal::new` over events), `report(&Journal, since)` and `score(state_dir, forecast)`, each returning the text; `main.rs` is the clap parser, the stdout write and the exit statuses (0, 1 with `togi-tool-stats: <error>` on stderr, 2 on a usage error). `just stats-rs` runs it next to the Go `stats` recipe, which is unchanged.

- **Design calls.**
  - It projects the journal's events directly (`togi-journal`'s `Recording`, so an unfinished last line is ignored and a journal that does not follow itself is an error). It does not use `togi-facts`, which validates what a tuning session needs (one decisive end per trial, a signal on every failure) and clones every event to do it; a review tool over a damaged journal should still report. `togi-facts` is not a dependency, against the Go package's import of `internal/facts`.
  - It reads no trial samples. A Rust `trial.end` carries the request telemetry the session summarized from the samples when it ended the trial, after a crash too, so Go's recovery of telemetry for ruleset-8 ends has nothing left to do. `togi-requests` supplies the top request; `togi-tuner` supplies `Profile` and `Topology` for the depth comparisons the evidence check needs, in place of `tuner.AtLeastDeep`.
  - The report is built as one string and written once, so a failed write is one error and exit status 1 and never a half report; Go kept the first write error of a section-by-section stream.
  - Tables are grouped by the vocabulary's own order (`R1`..`R7`, the activities and outcomes as declared), not by the alphabetical order of Go's strings. The Time table groups by the trial's activity, which replaces Go's phase. The R7 backoff report lists every backoff of a multi-core R7 failure: the Go ruleset-9 gate is gone with the Go rulesets. Legacy fallbacks for journals older than the profile and the cores of an intent (an absent profile, an R6 or R7 trial with no cores loading every core) are gone with the Go journals.
  - Goldens are insta snapshots of journals the tests build, so a tuner change leaves them alone; two tests over a simulated hunting session (cores 1 and 3 crash together) check the report against the journal's own events, not against bytes.
- **Go bugs fixed.** Go listed rulesets sorted as strings, so ruleset 10 sorted before 5; Rust sorts numerically (`rulesets_are_listed_in_numeric_order_and_builds_by_name`). Go printed an empty cell for the regime of a failure that names neither a regime nor a trial; Rust prints `-` (`a_failure_that_names_neither_a_regime_nor_a_trial_shows_dashes`). Go printed an inconclusive trial's reason raw, so a multi-line reason broke the table; Rust escapes it (`a_combination_failure_is_reported_in_every_section`) and prints `-` when there is none (`an_inconclusive_trial_without_a_reason_shows_a_dash`). Go sorted trial classes by their label text, so `120s` listed before `60s`; Rust orders them by catalog workload, loaded cores and numeric duration (`class_tables_list_workloads_in_catalog_order_and_durations_numerically`, `r7_request_tables_list_durations_numerically`), and prints hunt and cycle numbers unpadded.
- **Spec.** `docs/reviewing.md` gains "The Rust tool" with every difference from the Go output above; the Go text is unchanged until stage 9.
- **Tests.** 57 tests (the unit tests, 9 over the binary and a simulated session) run in about 1.0 s wall under `cargo nextest run -p togi-tool-stats`, against 0.2 s for `go test -count=1 ./tools/stats` (27 tests). Library 2,167 lines and tests 2,934 lines plus 575 of snapshots, against Go's 1,487 and 1,073.
- **Not measured.** Compile times against Go, agent cost, and the run on a Mac (the crate is clippy-clean for `aarch64-apple-darwin` from Linux).

## Stage 7: the whole workspace at dev opt-level 1 (#859, 2026-10-12)

[#845](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/845) built only dependencies, `togi-journal` and `togi-tuner` at `opt-level = 1`. That left `togi-machine`, `togi-sim`, `togi-session` and `togi-simrun` at 0, and three simulator tests took 30 to 60 s in CI's `rust-nextest`: `persistence::default_machine_1` (`togi-simrun`), and `resume::a_session_resumed_in_a_new_process_ends_where_an_uninterrupted_one_does` and `session::samples_reach_the_state_directory_only_when_asked_for` (`togi-tool-sim`). Profiles showed the time in `togi-machine`'s unoptimized helpers (`CoreId::from_index`, the `CoreSet` iterator) and in the inlined slice-bound checks ([#859](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/859)).

- **Change.** `[profile.dev] opt-level = 1` for the whole workspace replaces #845's three package overrides. Debug assertions, overflow checks and `debug = "line-tables-only"` are unchanged, as are the release and profiling profiles.
- **Measurements.** These were taken on the 32-thread host at load 12 to 24, with other agents running.

  One default session (`togi-tool-sim --state-dir <new dir>`, `taskset -c 0`, hyperfine, 3 runs):

  | | before | after |
  |---|---|---|
  | one default session | 4.67 ± 0.07 s | 1.30 ± 0.02 s |

  The three tests, each run alone three times (`cargo nextest run --profile ci -E <test>`):

  | test | before | after |
  |---|---|---|
  | `persistence::default_machine_1` | 5.3, 5.9, 7.3 s | 1.6, 1.6, 1.6 s |
  | `resume::a_session_resumed_…` | 12.3, 9.8, 9.5 s | 2.7, 2.6, 2.7 s |
  | `session::samples_reach_…` | 10.4, 9.9, 10.2 s | 3.0, 3.0, 3.6 s |

  Incremental `cargo build --tests -p togi-tool-sim -p togi-session` after a one-line change (hyperfine, 5 runs, 1 warmup):

  | changed crate | before | after |
  |---|---|---|
  | `togi-session` | 3.47 ± 0.53 s | 5.95 ± 0.31 s |
  | `togi-machine` | 10.17 ± 3.04 s | 11.88 ± 0.10 s |
- **Coverage.** `just cover-rs` still reports changed lines correctly at opt-level 1. A throwaway function was added to `togi-machine` and to `togi-session`, with a test that took one branch, and then dropped. For each crate the tool reported exactly the two lines of the untaken branch.
- **CI.** The pull request records the `rust-nextest` times before and after. The CI profile prints a test's time only when it passes 30 s, so the CI figures are bounds.

## Stage 4: closing measurements (#746, 2026-10-12)

Stage 4 ([#639](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/639)) ported `internal/tuningboot`, `internal/sim`, `tools/trialfacts`, `internal/session`, `internal/simrun`, `tools/sim` and the journal checks of `tools/audit` in six pull requests: [#748](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/748) (`togi-tuningboot`), [#773](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/773) (`togi-sim`), [#790](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/790) (`togi-tool-trialfacts`), [#810](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/810) (`togi-session`), [#812](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/812) (`togi-simrun`, with the tuner's simulator tests of #741) and [#813](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/813) (`togi-tool-sim` and `togi-tool-audit`). Three more fixed what the stage found on the way: [#804](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/804) commits a journal event exactly as its line decodes ([#800](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/800)), [#845](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/845) brings the session's crash sweeps within CI's test time limit ([#842](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/842)), and [#849](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/849) hands the simulation observer each event instead of rereading the journal ([#848](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/848)). This entry closes the stage with the measurements #637 asks for.

- **Test mapping.** [`rust-migration-stage-4-tests.md`](rust-migration-stage-4-tests.md) maps each of the 358 Go test functions of the seven packages, with `forecast_sim_test.go` and `memo_test.go` of `internal/tuner`, to the Rust tests that pin the same behavior: 329 are pinned by a test of the port's own crate, 11 by another crate's, 11 are obsolete under the native contract (each with its reason) and 7 are pinned by none: six `tools/audit` tests of the bench sweep and its run records, which move with the bench to [#642](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/642), and Go's benchmark of the dashboard projection and forecast over a simulated journal. Every Rust name resolves to a test that `cargo nextest list` reports on `97eba4cc` (`main` after #813) with this pull request's three tests added.
- **Three tests added.** `TestTuningBootReasonSurvivesResumedDeadEnd` had only its failure paths pinned. `togi_session::boot::tests::a_resumed_dead_end_acknowledges_the_pending_tuning_boot_reason_once_and_leaves_its_own` pins that a resumed dead end whose earlier tuning-boot leave reason is still pending acknowledges that reason in the journal once, leaves its own reason in the bootloader (or none once the saved entry was cleared), and that the next boot records no crash for it, whether nothing, the saved-entry event or the acknowledgement had been recorded before the resume; it fails when the acknowledgement is removed. `togi_session::boot::tests::a_resumed_dead_end_whose_saved_entry_append_fails_keeps_the_acknowledgement_and_its_own_reason` ports Go's fourth case: a saved-entry append that fails returns the journal error, keeps the acknowledgement once and leaves the dead end's own reason; it fails when the reason is cleared after that append fails. `togi_sim::suite::trials::a_replayed_crash_resets_by_watchdog_whatever_the_models_reset_mix` pins the case of `TestReplayCrashRecoveryPreservesFact` where a ThermalTrip-only reset mix must not change a replayed crash; it fails when the replay draws its reset from the model. No library change was needed.

**Host and method.** The development host: 32 threads, 30.9 GiB, Linux; `/tmp` is tmpfs and every build ran there. rustc and cargo 1.99.0, mold 2.42.1, cargo-nextest 0.9.146, go 1.27.1, hyperfine 1.21.0. Stage 3's script, on a `git archive` of `42a34414` for the first five crates and of `97eba4cc` for `togi-tool-sim` and `togi-tool-audit` (the five crates do not differ between the two), with a private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` and Go build cache and `GOFLAGS=-mod=mod`, after building each crate and package once. Every batch is hyperfine `--warmup 1 --runs 10` with JSON export, the Rust and the Go command in one invocation, back to back. **The host was loaded and shared**: other agents' builds and tests ran throughout, and no quiet window came in about an hour and a half of waiting for a one-minute load average below 3. The load average was 15.7 to 39.5 at the start and end of the batches. The comparable figure is therefore CPU time, the mean user plus system time of the command and its children over the runs, which a busy host disturbs far less than wall time; wall time (median, with the minimum) is given as indicative only. hyperfine exports the CPU time as a mean only, so it has no spread. On these trees the dev profile keeps line tables only and builds dependencies, `togi-journal` and `togi-tuner` at `opt-level = 1` (#845), while the measured crate itself compiles at `opt-level = 0`; stage 3 measured before that change, so the two stages' Rust figures are not comparable. [#860](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/860) later set `opt-level = 1` for the whole workspace, so on later trees the measured crate itself also builds and runs its tests at opt-level 1; these figures predate it.

The small crates cost about what Go costs, plus nextest's process per test, and the session suite 1.2 times Go's. `togi-simrun` and `togi-tool-sim` cost 5.7 and 23.6 times Go's: both run whole simulated sessions in their tests, and `togi-tool-sim` has 73 tests against 10 Go functions, its integration tests running the binary.

### Failures

`check.yml` on the nine pull requests' branches (`gh run list` and `gh run view --json jobs`): 38 runs, 29 successes, 5 failures and 4 cancelled, each cancelled run superseded by a newer push to the same branch. Every pull request's final head passed.

- Two failures on #810 (`b3723d66`, `dec914d9`): `togi-trial`'s `an_output_flood_neither_skips_load_steps_nor_delays_the_deadline` failed in `rust-nextest`, a crate #810 does not touch; the next push passed.
- Three failures on #812 (`56eb87b0`, `2d9aa542`) and #813 (`70718960`): `togi-session`'s `killing_the_run_at_every_durable_event_resumes_to_the_same_final_state` hit nextest's 120 s limit on the CI runner. That is #842, which #845 fixed.
- Failures caught before a push by the local gate are recorded nowhere and are not measured.

From each pull request's review records (the bot's machine-readable summaries; a re-review posts a new record with the findings of its own round, and these are summed). A row counts one finding by one reviewer, not one distinct defect.
