- The P1 in #773: Go's `TestSamplePersistenceFailuresRemainErrors` was mapped to a test that failed only when the sample directory or file could not be created; nothing proved that a failed write after opening, or a failed buffered flush, keeps the I/O error and the trial's exposure.
- The seven P1s in #810: a crash at the final kernel boundary was not latched before the state was published; recorded defects whose id the registry lacks were not handled; a crash returned by the ranking seam was not propagated; and four missing tests: a journal failure followed by a failed workload teardown, partial-start containment with the proof withheld on a wait error, and the same-boot interruption matrix at durable events and at the restoration boundaries without emergency cleanup.
- The P1 in #813: the test that the log fails to flush to `/dev/full` ran on every platform; it now runs on Linux only.
- The two rejections were each accepted by their reviewer: in #773, a crashed trial drops a sample-persistence error from `wait`, because on a real machine the workers die with it and recovery reads what is on disk; in #812, the assertion of no SMU write after a power loss can fail, and fails without the observer's power loss.
- Pre-review passes fixed findings before review opened: 7 listed in #810's body and 7 in #812's, each with a test that failed before the fix.
- #813 had three review rounds and #845 two; the others one.

### Agent cost

The harness's recorded usage of every assistant message, summed per transcript up to 2026-10-12T12:42:24Z: the stage's agent trees (four coordinators in turn and all their children: scouts, writers, pre-reviewers, the review coordinators, reviewers and fixers), the review coordinator shared by #804 to #849 with its reviewers and fixers, and two agents on #813's suite check (whether it runs in CI, $3.23, and the suite's per-session coverage, $1.41); the main session excluded. Cost is the recorded `cost.total` at the harness's configured prices, not an invoice.

| Model | Messages | Input tokens | Output tokens | Cache read | Cache write | Cost [USD] |
|:---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `anthropic/claude-opus-5-5` | 4,906 | 197,631 | 2,984,409 | 705,727,760 | 18,918,474 | 352.97 |
| `anthropic/claude-sonnet-5-5` | 2,589 | 5,305 | 2,591,388 | 385,269,711 | 10,725,354 | 107.35 |
| `openai-codex/gpt-6.1-sol` | 1,836 | 10,407,435 | 751,711 | 114,177,920 | 0 | 39.75 |
| **total** | **9,331** | **10,610,371** | **6,327,508** | **1,205,175,391** | **29,643,828** | **500.07** |

- Cache reads are 96.3% of the 1,251,757,098 recorded tokens. The nine merged pull requests and the work before them cost $477.80; this closing issue had cost $22.28 at the cut-off and is still open.
- Review (each pull request's review coordinator with its reviewers and fixers): $186.92 over 3,973 messages: #748 $4.82, #773 $39.00, #790 $8.11, #804 $1.65, #810 $48.88, #812 $27.10, #813 $27.25, #845 $0.73, #849 $1.47, and $27.92 of the coordinator's own work after 10:20Z, when it reviewed #812, #813, #845 and #849 together and its messages cannot be split between them. One review coordinator served #804 to #849; its reviewers and fixers are assigned by name and brief, and its own messages by time. Pre-review passes cost $3.17, and the fixer of their findings for #810 $5.08, outside that figure.
- Side calls the harness records separately (task-completion probes and small classifier and search calls): 3,948 calls, $1.26, not in the totals.
- The main session coordinated every stage in parallel, so its cost is not split by stage and is not counted.

### Wall time

From the first "Started on branch" comment on each pull request's issue to its merge: #748 0.52 h, #773 2.50 h, #790 0.61 h, #804 9.31 h, #810 16.29 h, #812 1.69 h, #813 2.78 h, #845 0.85 h, #849 0.48 h. #804 and #810 span the same overnight stop of 8.70 h as stage 3 (22:46:47Z to 07:28:52Z), which leaves 0.61 h and 7.59 h. #812 and #813 were claimed when their pull requests were stacked, at 09:41Z, while their writers had started at 22:25Z and 21:16Z the evening before; from the writers' start they took 4.25 h and 6.50 h without the stop. The stage ran from the first claim to the last merge in 21.26 h, 12.56 h without the stop; the pull requests overlapped, so their times do not add, and claims that came after writing began make these lower bounds.

### Limits

- One host under heavy sibling load (one-minute load average 15.7 to 39.5 at the batch boundaries), so the wall times can be several times what a quiet host gives and are indicative only; CPU time is less disturbed but still includes cache and frequency effects of a busy machine. The run on a quiet host is still [#796](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/796). `/tmp` is tmpfs, so disk speed is in no figure. Nothing ran on a Mac or on a GitHub runner.
- Ten runs per command; hyperfine reports CPU time as a mean only, so it has no spread, and every wall-time spread is a small-sample estimate. hyperfine ran the compile commands and every prepare step through a shell.
- The Go compile equivalent (a changed file in the package) is not the same operation as `cargo clean -p`, and the crates are not the same size as their packages (the Rust lines, library and tests together, are 1.7 to 7.8 times Go's).
- Agent cost: figures are at the cut-off. The trees are selected by name, so scouts and design work that served several pull requests are counted with them, and only review is split by pull request. The shared review coordinator's messages after 10:20Z are not split. The session was killed twice during the stage (out of memory, as stage 3's entry describes); a response in flight at a kill was never written, so its usage is missing. Local gate failures are recorded nowhere and are not measured.

## Stage 7: `cmd/togi` argument parsing, exits, version and completion (#825, 2026-10-12)

The `togi` crate (package `togi`, binary `togi`, library `togi_cli`) is the first of the four pull requests for `cmd/togi` ([#825](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/825)). It holds the clap parser, the typed exit statuses, `--version` and `togi completion bash|zsh|fish|nushell`; the other commands are not stubbed and arrive with the pull requests that implement them ([#826](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/826), [#828](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/828), [#829](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/829)). The crate's docs say how: one `Args` struct in a module, one `Command` variant and one arm of `Command::run`.

- **Design calls.**
  - `Exit` is one enum with a value for each status of the spec's table, including the doctor and dead-end codes 10 to 18, and `Exit::dead_end(DeadEndCondition)` is an exhaustive match. `main` is `run(args, stdout, stderr).into()`; `run` takes the streams as parameters, so the tests that call it directly cover the same code as the ones that run the binary.
  - `--state-dir` is `global = true` on the top-level parser, so every command takes it before or after its name. `--config` is `ConfigArgs`, flattened by `doctor`, `run` and `reset`; no other command has it. Go accepted `--config` before the command name too (`flags.go:30` registers it on the top-level flag set, `main.go:117` rejects it for a command that does not take it), and so does the spec; clap scopes a flag to its command, so `config_after_command` moves a leading `--config <path>` or `--config=<path>` to just after the command name before clap parses, and an unknown `--config` anywhere, with a command or without one, gets the spec's usage error `--config applies only to doctor, run and reset` (exit 2) in place of clap's unexpected-argument text.
  - A parse error is rendered by `clap::Error::render` and written by `run`, not printed by clap, so the exit status is `Exit::Usage` or `Exit::Ok` from the same enum and a failed write of the output is an error. clap's help, `-h`, `-V` and `help <command>` are kept as clap has them.
  - Flags without a command: `togi --state-dir /x` is clap's `MissingSubcommand`, which `report` words as the no-argument case (the wordmark and clap's help on stderr, exit 2), as the spec says `togi` without a command does; `togi --config x` stays the scope error. The command position for that error comes from the same scan that moves `--config` (`split_leading`), so a value of `--config` or `--state-dir` that is also a command name (`togi --config completion`) is not taken for the command.
  - The scripts come from `clap_complete` and `clap_complete_nushell`, written to stdout in one piece. The shells are an own `ValueEnum` of the four the spec names; clap's elvish and PowerShell are not offered.
  - The nushell script is loaded with `nu` by one test, which fails when `nu` is not on `PATH` rather than passing without loading anything; the dev shell and the flake's `rust-nextest` check provide `nu`.
- **Go bugs fixed.** Go ignored every write error (`_, _ = io.WriteString(...)`), so a full stdout cut a completion script or the help short and still exited 0; Rust exits 1 with the error on stderr (`a_script_that_cannot_be_written_is_an_error_not_a_panic`, `help_that_cannot_be_written_is_an_error`, and over the binary on `/dev/full`, `a_stdout_that_cannot_be_written_exits_1_with_the_error_on_stderr`). A stdout closed when the process starts is not fixed: Rust's runtime reopens fd 1 on `/dev/null` before `main` (seen with `strace`: `poll` reports `POLLNVAL` for fd 1, then `openat("/dev/null")` returns 1), so the output is discarded and the status is 0, as in Go; the spec's `completion` row says so. `deadEndExit` returned 1 for a condition it did not list, so a new dead end would have exited as a plain error without a test failing; the Rust match has no default arm, so a new `DeadEndCondition` does not compile until it has a status.
- **Spec.** `docs/spec/runtime.md` (the `completion` row and Shared flags, not the `--config` placement, which is as the spec has it): clap's error text and usage hint in place of Go's `flag provided but not defined` text; `-h` and `-V`; `help <command>`; the revision from `TOGI_REV`, with no VCS stamping, set by the flake's Rust package once it exists ([#830](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/830)) while `packages.default` stays the Go build with its own stamp; `togi` with only `--state-dir` prints the wordmark, and `--config` without a command is the scope error even with `--help` or `--version`; a failed write is exit 1 and a stdout closed at start is discarded with exit 0; completion generated from the clap definition, so it offers `-h` and `-V` and the shell names, bash completes a flag's value only after a space, and the comma-separated kinds of `events --kind` arrive with `events` ([#826](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/826)).
- **Tests.** 48 tests (32 unit, 16 over the binary) with 8 snapshots (4 completion, 2 help, 2 error text) in `crates/togi/tests/cli/snapshots` (406 lines), reviewed against the spec. The Go tests of the files in scope, with `cmd/togi/testdata/completion-*.golden` and `help-completion.golden`:

| Go test | Rust |
|---|---|
| `TestTopLevelRefusals` | `cli::tests::no_command_prints_the_wordmark_and_usage_to_stderr_and_exits_2`, `cli::tests::state_dir_without_a_command_prints_the_wordmark_and_usage_to_stderr_and_exits_2`, `cli::tests::usage_errors_exit_2_with_stderr_only`, and over the binary `no_command_prints_the_wordmark_and_usage_on_stderr` (snapshot), `state_dir_without_a_command_prints_the_wordmark_and_usage_on_stderr`, `usage_errors_exit_2_on_stderr_with_nothing_on_stdout`, `a_stdout_that_cannot_be_written_exits_1_with_the_error_on_stderr` (`/dev/full`) |
| `TestVersion` | `cli::tests::version_is_the_togi_version_on_stdout_only`, `cli::tests::version_after_a_command_is_a_usage_error`, `version_prints_the_version_and_revision_on_stdout`, `a_local_build_reports_the_dev_revision`, `version_after_a_command_is_a_usage_error` (binary), `completion_reads_and_writes_nothing_in_the_state_directory`; the `status` and `run` rows of Go's table move to [#826](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/826) and [#829](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/829) with those commands |
| `TestVersionInTopLevelUsageOnly` | `top_level_help` and `completion_help` snapshots (`--version` only in the first, `--state-dir` in both) |
| `TestHelpExamplesShareOneDescriptionColumn` | `cli::tests::examples_start_every_description_in_one_column`, `cli::tests::every_help_examples_block_shares_one_description_column` (every command, so later ones are covered when added) |
| `TestCyclesExampleWordedOnce` | not here: it pins `run`'s example, in [#829](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/829) |
| `TestFlagErrorsSpellFlagsWithTwoDashes` | obsolete: it pinned the rewording of the `flag` package's messages; clap words its own, pinned in the usage-error tests above. The per-command rows go to the PRs of those commands |
| `TestFlagErrorsPreserveQuotedValues` | obsolete: the same rewording, whose parser could mistake a submitted value for its own text; clap's errors carry the value as data |
| `TestConfigOnCommandsWithoutConfigIsAUsageError`, `TestReadOnlyCommandsRejectConfig`, `TestLeadingConfigRejectionPrecedesCommandArguments` | `cli::tests::config_is_rejected_with_the_scope_error_wherever_it_is_written` (before and after the name, `--config=` and space forms, no command, with `--help`, `--version` or a command name as its value, exact message, exit 2), `the_scope_error_shows_the_usage_of_the_command_it_names` (not for `--config completion`), `args::tests::config_is_rejected_where_it_is_not_attached`, the `--config` rows of the binary's usage-error test; the commands' own rows come with them |
| `TestCommandHelpConfigScope`, `TestWriteCommandsAcceptConfigPlacements` | `args::tests::config_is_accepted_before_and_after_the_command_name_it_is_attached_to`, `cli::tests::a_leading_config_moves_after_the_command_name`, `other_arguments_are_left_as_they_are`, `args::tests::config_defaults_and_reports_whether_it_was_given`, `args::tests::the_config_help_names_the_default_path`, `completion::tests::only_the_commands_that_read_the_configuration_offer_it` |
| `TestCompletionScripts` and the four `completion-*.golden` | `bash_completion`, `zsh_completion`, `fish_completion`, `nushell_completion` snapshots |
| `TestCompletionCoversCommands` | its command-name and flag checks are `completion::tests::every_script_completes_the_command_and_its_shared_flag` and `cli::tests::the_definition_is_consistent` (clap's `debug_assert`); clap generates the scripts from the definition, so the hand-kept template it checked is gone. Its kind-list check (`events --kind`) moves to [#826](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/826) with `events` |
| `TestCompletionExitCodes` and `help-completion.golden` | `usage_errors_exit_2_with_stderr_only`, `a_bad_shell_lists_the_supported_ones` (the shell list appears only for an unknown shell), `a_missing_shell_error_names_the_argument` (snapshot; it names `<SHELL>` only), `state_dir_before_or_after_the_command_is_accepted`, `completion_help` (snapshot) |
| `TestNushellCompletionLoads` | `nushell_loads_the_script_without_warnings` (asserts the shells are offered); its `events --kind se` → `session` assertion moves to [#826](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/826) with `events` |
| `TestRunResultExitCodes` | its second loop (`run_test.go:322-345`, `deadEndExit` for each dead-end condition and `"unknown"` → 1) maps to `exit::tests::dead_ends_map_by_name` and `exit::tests::each_dead_end_has_its_own_code_between_10_and_18` (the Rust match has no default arm, so the `"unknown"` row has no counterpart); its first loop, `runResult`'s outcomes and diagnostics, moves to [#829](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/829) with `run` |
| `golden` helper (`golden_test.go`) | obsolete: insta |

  `TestStatusFitsEightyColumns`, `TestWrapLines`, `TestStatusHuntLabelsAccumulatedPassEvidence` and `TestStatusWideTablesWrapWithoutDroppingValues` sit in `cli_text_test.go` but pin `status`; they map in [#826](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/826).
- **Measurements** (host load 1.1 at the start, hyperfine 1.21.0, `/tmp`, a private Go build cache, `GOFLAGS=-mod=mod`; CPU is user plus system, the mean of the runs):

| Operation | Rust CPU [ms] | Rust wall [ms] | Go CPU [ms] | Go wall [ms] |
|:---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Build, `cargo clean -p togi` then `cargo build -p togi` (5 runs, dependencies built) against `go build ./cmd/togi` after appending a comment to `main.go` (5 runs) | 538 | 308 ± 4 | 772 | 274 ± 8 |
| Tests, `cargo nextest run -p togi` (40 tests when measured, built) against `go test -count=1 -tags integration -run <the mapped tests> ./cmd/togi` (105 test and subtest runs, the test binary cached; 10 runs each) | 330 | 126 ± 7 | 339 | 160 ± 5 |

  Library and binary come to 528 lines and the tests to 587 plus 255 over the binary, against Go's 800 lines for `main.go`, `flags.go`, `exit.go` and `completion.go` (which also hold the registry of the commands not yet ported) and 824 for the seven test files.
- **Not measured.** The time to compile the test binaries from clean, the Go package's whole test run, agent cost, and the run on a Mac (the crate is clippy-clean for `aarch64-apple-darwin` from Linux; the scripts were not loaded in a shell other than nushell 0.115.1).

## Stage 7: `tools/bench` (a) (#821, 2026-10-12)

The `togi-tool-bench` crate ports the runs, metrics, summary, comparison and cache of `tools/bench` ([#821](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/821)); the gates ([#822](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/822)) and `--same`, `--smoke` and `--forecast` ([#823](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/823)) stay with the Go bench, and the module and record boundaries leave room for them (the gate is kept as raw JSON in the suite, and a run has a `smoke` flag). It builds `togi-tool-sim` from the tree with cargo, copies the binary into a private directory and starts the copy as a subprocess per run, as Go builds `tools/sim` into a temporary directory; `--sim PATH` replaces it. `just bench-rs` runs it; the Go recipes are unchanged. 3,764 lines of library with its unit tests and 3,517 of tests (159 tests, 6 insta snapshots), against Go's 1,407 lines in `main.go`, `metrics.go`, `compare.go`, `cache.go` and `suite_notes.go` (which also dispatch the gate and `--same` modes) and 1,524 in the in-scope test files.

- **Own formats.** Records, summary, comparison and cache are Rust's: a strict record with every field required except the three that may be absent, `model_check` reduced to counts, `evidence` and `ruleset` from `togi_tuner`. The Go `baseline.jsonl` is not rewritten. The cache key binds the hash of the simulator copy that runs, the bench's own executable's hash, `--max-boots`, the suite file, each machine file with its overlays and facts extract, and the run's identity; it lives in `togi/bench-rs`, apart from Go's `togi/bench`, whose `costs.json` differs.
- **Deliberate differences.** Overlay machines extend by the same rules the simulator reads them with. Records are in suite order whatever `--jobs` is. Every scenario's machines load whatever the split selects, so the model checks also cover machines only unselected runs use (none in the committed suite). A session a signal killed or stopped is an uncached `error` record. The final profile is the last `profile.change` (the tuner's checking profile, which only that event sets) rather than a tuner replay, so a hand-built journal can be measured; on the 220 suite runs the records are identical to the replayed ones.
- **Go bugs fixed.** The comparison's warning about unmatched runs subtracted the pairs from the baseline runs in the candidate's splits, which went negative when a paired run had changed split and hid an unmatched run behind it; both sides are now counted directly.
- **Sanity benchmark.** `togi-tool-bench sanity` compares per-metric means of a Rust run of all 220 runs (dev and holdout) with `tools/bench/baseline.jsonl` (Go ruleset 11, recorded at `5145f657`; no tuner or simulator change in Go since). The band per metric is in `sanity.rs`: a line is flagged when the means differ by more than an absolute floor and a relative share of Go's mean (5% for hours and trials, 3% for depth) and by more than 3 standard errors (Welch per scenario, stratified by scenario for the total), when concluded runs were lost, or when one side lacks a metric the other measured. 220 of 220 runs concluded; 2 of 149 comparisons are flagged. Whole-suite means, Rust against Go:

  | Metric | Rust | Go | Δ | z |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | concluded | 1.0000 | 1.0000 | 0 | 0.00 |
  | sim_hours | 59.5356 | 59.7824 | −0.4% | −0.57 |
  | first_passed_cycle_h | 39.2914 | 39.6406 | −0.9% | −1.10 |
  | passed_cycles | 2.0000 | 2.0000 | 0 | 0.00 |
  | crashes | 115.4636 | 117.4182 | −1.7% | −1.17 |
  | trials | 1717.6682 | 1720.2318 | −0.1% | −0.21 |
  | trial_hours | 54.7243 | 54.9058 | −0.3% | −0.44 |
  | hunts | 26.4909 | 26.3182 | +0.7% | 0.28 |
  | combinations | 0.7818 | 0.6227 | +25.5% | 1.60 |
  | depth | −518.2318 | −515.6864 | −0.5% | −1.18 |
  | real_answer_share | 0.1450 | 0.1425 | +1.7% | 2.48 |
  | worst_r7_hazard_per_h (96 runs) | 3.0275 | 3.0224 | +0.2% | 0.02 |
  | **hazard_max_per_h** | 0.3545 | 1.1415 | −68.9% | −3.31 (flagged) |

  `hazard_max_per_h` is the largest steady-state hazard of a run's final profile: mostly 0, with a few runs ending one step past a limit at 10 to 16 per hour. Of the committed `target` and three R7 scenarios (116 runs), 6 Rust and 20 Go runs end above 1 per hour. The R7 scenarios share a base machine and seeds, so one seed lands in the tail of each and the runs are not independent. Fresh seeds 2001 to 2200 of `target` and the three R7 scenarios (800 runs a side) give 65 Rust and 50 Go runs above 1 per hour (Fisher p 0.18), and a stratified z of +1.60 for the mean, Rust higher; pooled with the suite, 71 against 70. Go's committed seeds are the outlier against Go's own fresh seeds (20 of 116 against 50 of 800, p 0.0002). On the 200 fresh `target` runs and the 172 suite runs with a machine file, Go's hazard model recomputed on the Rust final profile gives Rust's `hazard_max_per_h` to a relative 1e-9, so neither the metric nor the model differs there (each bench draws its own `default` machine, so those runs were not compared); the flag is the committed seeds' tail. The second flag, `misleading-mce` `first_passed_cycle_h` (8 runs a side, 30.47 against 35.75 hours, z −3.15, a two-sided p of about 0.012 under Student's t, about 1.8 such lines expected by chance among 149), is the same kind: fresh seeds 2001 to 2200 give 32.92 against 33.47 hours (Welch t −1.44, p 0.15), with sim_hours, depth and crashes inside p 0.11 too, and the committed samples sit in opposite tails of their own fresh distributions. No finding for another crate's owner.
- **Measured.** Release build, `--jobs 8`, a cold cache, 32-thread host at load 9.9 before and 9.2 after: 76.2 s wall for the 220 runs, 416 MB maximum resident set of the bench (an earlier run at load 33 to 40 took 119.7 s). After the review fixes the same run with `--no-cache` took 95 s on a busier host and wrote records equal to the earlier ones in every field but the wall times. `cargo nextest run -p togi-tool-bench` ran 159 tests in 1.8 s on a built tree, and `go test -count=1 ./tools/bench` took 0.96 s (including the gate, `--same` and forecast tests this crate does not port).
- **Review findings fixed.** Round 1 found that the run, CLI and sanity tests needed the checkout's Git metadata and `tools/bench/baseline.jsonl`, which the `rust-nextest` sandbox lacks (the tree stamp now comes in through the options, the CLI tests build their own repository and the check gets git and the baseline); that the signal watcher's blocked mask was inherited by cargo and every simulator, so Ctrl-C waited for them (a handler-based watcher now); that the bench hashed the shared `target/release` binary but ran it by path, so a rebuild during a bench poisoned the cache (a private copy now); that a session killed or stopped by a signal was cached, and a Ctrl-C reaching the simulators could cache a cut session as concluded (own process groups and no caching); that a run failure let the other simulators run to their end; that the sanity bands hid a 10% hours shift and a lost conclusion in a scenario of 24 or more runs, dropped a metric one side lacked and pooled the total's spread across scenarios; and that the Go metric, worst-R7, real-answer, cache-touch, cancellation and suite-note tests were mapped to Rust tests that did not pin their behavior. Most fixes come with a test shown to fail on the old code; the positional-array, `--jobs` ordering and split-selection tests were judged by reading.
- **Not measured.** Compile times against Go, agent cost, and the run on a Mac.

## Stage 6: the watch terminal loop (#836, 2026-10-12)

The `terminal` module of `togi-watch` ports the loop of `internal/watch` ([#836](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/836), part of [#641](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/641)): `terminal::run` and `terminal::run_after` show the live dashboard, `terminal::one_frame` prints one frame without a terminal, and the source, notification, key decoding, signals and `TERM=linux` palette are its private modules. Each public call drives its own current-thread tokio runtime and returns with no task and no thread still running. The loop is generic over a frame callback, so it is tested with fake frames and a recording ratatui backend under tokio's paused time; the frames of [#835](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/835) are composed with it in their own pull request.

- **What changed from Go.** ratatui diffs the buffer, so the row-diff writer, the per-row erase to end of line and the stale-row blanking are gone; the screen is cleared on entry and again on a resize or a view change, and the last row and column are never written (`docs/spec/runtime.md`, Dashboard, now says that). The journal is reloaded by file identity as before, but an append folds only its new events into the same model, and only while the last event the model folded is still in its place; a replacement, a truncation, a rewrite in place or a changed modification time at the same size builds a fresh one. The held log list is discarded on removal, replacement, truncation, a rewrite in place or a read error. A journal of a newer schema is a problem, judged with `Build::verdict` for `Operation::Inspect`. One queue carries keys, signals, resizes, journal changes and cancellation, so the loop reacts in the order things happened. SIGINT, SIGTERM and SIGHUP are installed by `run` itself (in Go, `cmd/togi` installed them); `Stop` lets another thread end a running dashboard, for the dashboard of `togi run`.
- **Go bugs fixed.** One, in the inotify adapter: the climb toward a missing state directory watches the first directory above it that exists, and a directory made between the failed watch below and that watch made no event anyone saw, so a `togi watch` started before its state directory chain was created could wait forever. The climb now looks again at the directory it last found missing once it has watched the one above, and climbs again if it now exists, at most once a level; `notify::linux::a_directory_made_while_the_climb_passes_it_is_still_watched` makes it between the two calls. Differences that are not bugs: a lone escape waits 50 ms of silence for the rest of a sequence, where Go anchored the 50 ms to the first fragment; the console palette is set only for the `Colours::Console` profile, so `TERM=linux` with `NO_COLOR` no longer redefines colours it will not use; the colour profile reads `TERM` only for `linux`, so `TERM=dumb` or an unset `TERM` no longer turns colour off as Go's colorprofile did, and only a non-empty `NO_COLOR` (or journald) does. The colour profile choice and the Linux console's colour table live in `terminal::palette`.
- **Notification.** inotify is used through `rustix` and tokio's `AsyncFd`, not the `notify` crate the issue names. The crate's mask includes `IN_OPEN`, which every reload of the journal would trigger, and its watcher thread is detached on drop, which the rule that each call returns with no thread still running rules out; the Go rules (watch the state directory and its parent, the journal alone when the directory is traverse-only, the nearest existing ancestor while it is missing, a once-a-second look only when nothing can be watched, one reload acknowledgement per change) are ported as they were. Nothing is looked for while the frame on screen is frozen. The non-Linux adapter polls once a second, also while frozen, because that is the only way it learns of a change.
- **Signals.** The dashboard listens through `tokio::signal`: `run` for SIGINT, SIGTERM, SIGHUP and SIGWINCH, and `run_after`, the dashboard of `togi run` (Go's `RunAfter`), for SIGWINCH only, taking its cancellation from a `Stop` handle. tokio cannot unregister a listener, so the listeners `run` made stay registered for the rest of the process: a later SIGINT, SIGTERM or SIGHUP no longer takes its default action, and SIGWINCH, which is ignored by default, behaves as before. Every command that shows the dashboard therefore keeps its own listener for SIGINT, SIGTERM and SIGHUP for the process's lifetime, as Go's `togi run` does through `signal.NotifyContext`. The owner decided this over `sigaction` handlers put back on exit, because those need `unsafe`, which the project allows only in the crates for `internal/trial` and `internal/hardware`. Two dashboards in one process both receive a signal, so neither waits for the other. The keyboard pipe is made close-on-exec in one call (`pipe2`) except on Apple platforms, which lack it.
- **Tests.** 101 unit tests (the loop under paused time with a recording backend, the reader on scripted and real pipes, the source on real files, the notifier with a fake watch under paused time, inotify on real directories, signals sent to the test process) and 22 `real_process_` tests, 19 of which run this test binary again on a pseudo-terminal: SIGINT, SIGTERM, SIGHUP and SIGWINCH on a frozen frame, keys, keys that are not a terminal, a SIGINT after `run` returns that neither kills the helper nor escapes a listener the helper makes afterwards, a second `run` in the process still ended by SIGINT, `run_after` leaving SIGINT, SIGTERM and SIGHUP to its caller's listeners, and real inotify through a running dashboard. The helper is killed on every path, and every wait is bounded: a helper must close its terminal within 30 s, a real pipe must deliver within 5 s, and a unit test waiting on a signal stops its dashboard or fails after 20 s. A wait on the pseudo-terminal resumes just past what it matched, so a frame that arrives in the same read as a marker is not skipped.
- **Review findings fixed.** Round 1 found 9 P2 and 16 P3 findings, all owned by the pull request. One P2 was a code defect: a journal rewritten in place to a longer one folded its tail onto the old model and kept the held log list. The other P2 were tests that passed without the code they named (the held-list drops on truncation and on a broken journal, the frozen notifier, stale-cell erasure, a repaint caused by SIGWINCH rather than the clock), a real-pipe test that raced the 50 ms escape timeout, helper waits that ended silently at their bound, and the unrecorded `unsafe` of the `sigaction` handlers, which the owner resolved by moving to `tokio::signal`. The P3 included the IN_Q_OVERFLOW path, the unreadable-ancestor rule, the pipes' close-on-exec race, tokio's then-unused `signal` feature, chmod tests that left unlistable directories on failure, the spec's palette sentence, and a second dashboard in the process that put the terminal into raw mode, flushing the first one's input, before it waited for the signals; that wait went with the `sigaction` handlers. Later, CI found two P2 races, both owned by the pull request: the pseudo-terminal wait that skipped a frame read together with a marker, and the climb race under Go bugs fixed.
- **Measurements.** Split at each file's `mod tests`, with `testing.rs` counted as tests, the terminal module holds about 1,920 lines of library and 3,630 of unit tests and test support, and `tests/real_process.rs` 926, against Go's 811 lines of `run.go`, `notify_linux.go` and `notify_other.go` and 1,506 of `run_test.go`, `source_test.go`, `notify_linux_test.go` and `journal_view_test.go`. The 123 tests take about 2.1 s under `cargo nextest run -p togi-watch -E 'test(/terminal::/) | binary(real_process)'`, 2 s of it one test waiting for two once-a-second frames, against about 0.11 s for the 28 ported Go tests under `go test -count=1 -run '^(…)$' ./internal/watch`.
- **Not measured.** Compile times against Go, and agent cost. Not run on a Mac: `aarch64-apple-darwin` is clippy-checked only, so the polling adapter, the pipe made without `pipe2` and the terminal size ioctl are untested there.
- **Limits.** An append reads the whole journal again and folds the new events (`Recording` has no read from an offset).

## Stage 7: `tools/bench` (b) (#822, 2026-10-12)

The `togi-tool-bench` crate gains the gate reports of `tools/bench` ([#822](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/822)): `gate.rs` types the suite's `gate` object, judges a run against a baseline and prints the report, and `bench-rs --baseline` prints it after the comparison and exits 1 unless the gate passes. `--same`, `--smoke` and `--forecast` stay with [#823](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/823).

- **Own format.** The gate object is typed and strict (an unknown key is refused), the bootstrap seed is one integer for a `StdRng`, and the report header says `seed N` where Go printed `PCG(a,b)`. The criteria, thresholds and refusals are Go's. `docs/benchmarking.md` has a new "The Rust gate" section.
- **Archived Go gates are not re-scored.** The Ruleset 11 archive's draws came from Go's PCG and its records are Go's. The Rust bench refuses a gate whose `bootstrap_seed` is a pair before any session runs, its record reader refuses the archive's records, and a test pins the four archive files by hash.
- **Independent of threads.** One generator per quantile criterion, drawn on one thread in a fixed order; a test computes the same interval from eight threads.
- **Go bugs fixed.** A scenario both gated and concluded printed its conclusion criterion twice (now refused); repeated names in `gated`, `pooled`, `conclude` and `quantiles` were accepted (now refused); the label `p%.0f` printed 0.995 as `p100` (labels keep their decimals, a repeated label is refused); `resamples` was unbounded (now 1 to 10,000,000); a time criterion with no pair that both sides concluded printed `ratio=0.0000` (now `ratio=none timed=0`, still a failure).
- **Size.** At its first review head, e81a82df, the pull request was 12 files, +1751 −11; after the review fixes it is 15 files, +1876 −62, the added lines mostly tests and the git fixture helpers moved out of `tests/cli.rs` into a module of their own. The Rust gate is `src/gate.rs` at 644 lines, with 819 lines of tests in `tests/gate.rs`, 259 in `tests/gate_cli.rs` and a 24-line snapshot. Go's is `tools/bench/gate.go` at 311 lines, with 384 lines of tests in `gate_test.go` and 128 in `gate_archive_test.go` (the archive test, which the Rust crate has no counterpart for beyond its hash pin), and 233 in the `integration`-tagged `gate_cli_integration_test.go`.
- **Measured.** The Rust gate tests, `cargo nextest run -p togi-tool-bench -E 'binary(gate) | binary(gate_cli)'` on a built tree in the dev profile, ran 28 tests (25 in `gate`, 3 in `gate_cli`) in 1.76 s in total; the three CLI tests took 0.26 s, 0.39 s and 1.76 s of it, and the pinned-interval test, four bootstraps of 10,000 resamples over 24 pairs, 0.05 s. Go's `go test -count=1 -run 'Gate|Archived|Committed' ./tools/bench` ran in 0.83 s (the `integration`-tagged gate CLI test is excluded, as the default `go test` excludes it; 22.6 s wall including the `nix develop` start and the test binary's compile). No gate run over the 220-run suite was timed: the suite registers no gate. Both on one host under sibling load, so indicative only.
- **Regressions.** None known: the Go bench is unchanged, and the pull request's other edits are small wiring in the crate's `main.rs`, `run.rs`, `suite.rs`, `lib.rs` and `error.rs`, one line of `tests/run.rs`, the move of the git fixture helpers, one line of `flake.nix` and the docs.
- **Review findings fixed.** The first review found 2 P1, 2 P2 and 3 P3. P1: the `gate_cli` tests were not hermetic in the sandboxed `nextest` check (no Git repository, and `tools/bench/gates` outside the source set). P2: the gate's baseline accepted a positional JSON array, and the bootstrap pin did not depend on the generator stream. P3: repeated pooled and concluded refusals were untested, a false docs claim that the Go bench can re-judge the archive, and incomplete measurements. The fix round found 2 more P3: the shared test module gained a `dead_code` allow (the git helpers now have their own module), and the size above named no commit. All are fixed in the pull request.
- **Not measured.** Compile times against Go, agent cost, and the run on a Mac.

## Stage 6: `togi-watch` frames (#835, 2026-10-12)

The `frames` module of `togi-watch` draws the `togi watch` dashboard ([#835](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/835), part of [#641](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/641)): `frames::render(&Journal, &Screen, now, &TimeZone, &mut Buffer) -> Drawn` into a ratatui buffer of `(width - 1) x (height - 1)`. It ports `story.go`, `render.go`, `panels.go`, `help.go` and `canvas.go` (4,609 lines): 12,110 lines of library and 6,357 of tests, with 90 insta snapshots, against Go's 3,030 lines of tests for those files and 65 goldens.

- **Shape.** A `Scene` carries one frame's snapshot, clock, zone and colour profile; a started idle (R6) trial puts the clock at the trial's start, and `render` returns `Hold::Frozen` for it and `Hold::Never` for every frame that shows the clock, so the loop knows nothing about R6. `measure` places the model-dependent panels; `story/` narrates, `dash/` draws the header, stage line, trial band, CCD tables and R7 lines, `panels/` the context panels, the history and the journal view. Colour is applied by `Paint` and the profile at drawing time; no ANSI string is parsed back as Go's canvas did. `frames::Tone` became `Paint`, and the model's `words::Tone` maps to it through one `From`. Raw facts are escaped at output. Help is a view of `render`; Go's header-less help entry is gone.
- **Go bugs fixed.** After a trial was over the CCD tables still marked its cores under load and the cycle panel kept its part running; an idle failure that was not a crash read as a crash in the hunt cause; a known idle failure printed an empty name; `tightNext` left a trailing space; "1 clean cycles"; the R7 top cell of a workload with no top requester read `no cores` instead of `-`.
- **Defects found before review.** The snapshot review found a dead end's stage line still marking stages as running and counting down the abandoned trial in the help and log summary, and a cycle part reading `6/4 passed` because the tuner counts rerun passes (the panel clamps to the quota); joining the slices found `note_line` unexported.
- **Wiring.** `terminal::run`, `terminal::run_after` and `terminal::one_frame` are the CLI's entry points: they draw with `frames::render` in the system's time zone, choose the colour profile with `frames::colours_for` and set the Linux console's palette from `frames::console_palette`, and the loop's stand-ins in `terminal/palette.rs` are gone. The versions that take a render callback are private to the terminal module, so the pseudo-terminal tests moved from `tests/real_process.rs` into the crate as `terminal::real_process`. Three of them run the real frames: through `run`, a started idle (R6) trial's frame draws nothing more for 2.5 s once it is on screen while the checking scenario's clock keeps redrawing, and `run_after` draws the starting frame.
- **Review findings fixed.** Round 1 found 37 issues (10 P2): the hunt panel called an idle failure that was not a crash a crash, the step row kept `trial 1 of 1` after a stop, the SOLO LIMITS story had no brief so 160x45 cut its offset, the compact outcome line took its request basis from the all-pass row, a cut finished cycle counted the blank under its rule in `+N more`, a finished cycle read `paused at step 18 of 18`, one-frame snapshots were drawn with the key footer, help still glossed record-only trials, and the frame-time comparison set a buffer fill against Go's styled strings; the rest were test gaps and tests that could not fail.
- **Spec.** `docs/spec/runtime.md` says the BIOS rule names the journal event that confirmed or unconfirmed the profile (`#N`), not a cycle number or a time, and that the phase 2 context opens with the rounds left, as the native tuner records it.
- **Frame time.** `cargo bench -p togi-watch --bench frames` (criterion) on the development host, which was running other builds: `frame-bytes`, a 240x67 frame drawn and encoded as the bytes a first draw writes (what Go's `RenderView` produced as styled strings), takes 0.60 ms for the longest scenario (hunt-bios, 11,230 events) and 0.54 ms for the first R7 checking trial; drawing into the buffer alone takes 0.27 and 0.32 ms; folding the checking journal takes 0.84 ms. Go on the same host, `go test -bench` with a scratch `RenderView` benchmark that was not committed: 1.17 ms (hunt-bios, 11,625 events, since the Go simulator's journal differs) and 2.05 to 2.31 ms (checking); `BenchmarkProject` 4.1 to 6.3 ms.
- **Tests.** Every Go test of `frames_test.go`, `render_test.go` (except `TestPress` and `TestDecodeKeys`, the loop's), `fit_test.go`, `r7_status_test.go`, `story_test.go`, `narration_test.go`, `cycle_resume_test.go`, `history_render_test.go` and `log_render_test.go`, and `TestHistoryPanelCountsEveryEntryItLeavesOut`, maps to a Rust test or a stated reason in the pull request. Every frame a test renders passes the bounds check. The crate's 569 tests take about 6.5 s under `cargo nextest run -p togi-watch`. `just cover-rs origin/main` lists 81 uncovered changed lines in `frames/`, 12 of them in test files, and two panic arms of the pseudo-terminal helpers.
- **Not measured.** Compile times, agent cost, and the frame time on the Linux console.

## Stage 7: the Rust package (#867, 2026-10-12)

The flake gains `packages.togi-rs`, the Rust `togi` in the release profile with its four completion scripts installed, and the check `rust-package`, which builds it with the revision `dev` and checks `--version` and that each completion script names `togi` ([#830](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/830), pull request [#867](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/867)). `packages.default` stays the Go build until the cutover ([#643](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/643)). `togi-rs` and the simulator binary of the `rust-sim-suite-<i>` checks share one dependency derivation, `togi-release-deps` (crane's `buildDepsOnly` with `-p togi -p togi-tool-sim`), which replaces the simulator's own `togi-tool-sim-deps`. Compared with the crates `togi-tool-sim-deps` built in #861's CI log, `togi-release-deps` in #867's log also builds `clap_complete`, `clap_complete_nushell`, `insta`, `similar` and `togi` itself (the `Checking` and `Compiling` lines of shard 0 in the two logs).

- **Package size.** `nix path-info -s -S` on the outputs of `nix build --no-link --print-out-paths .#togi-rs .#default` at `3f3e78a7`, the pull request's head merged with `main`:

  | package | output | closure |
  |---|---:|---:|
  | `togi-rs` (Rust) | 1,360,000 B | 50,611,928 B |
  | `default` (Go) | 12,690,568 B | 53,058,064 B |

  The Rust binary holds only `--version`, `--help` and `completion` so far, so its size says nothing yet about the finished command. `nix path-info -r` lists glibc and the `xgcc` libgcc in both closures, the gcc 16.2.0 `libgcc` and `lib` in Rust's only, and tzdata in Go's only.
- **Build in one store.** From `nix log` of the local builds. `togi-release-deps` runs three cargo passes, `cargo check --release --all-targets`, `cargo build --release` and `cargo test --release --no-run`, whose `Finished` lines read 2.54 s, 2.98 s and 3.17 s (a log read from the Nix daemon; the derivation was not rebuilt). With it in the store, one `nix build` of `.#togi-rs` and of the simulator's `togi-tool-sim` derivation, the two building concurrently, compiled workspace crates only and no dependency: `togi-rs` 6 crates, `Finished` in 7.10 s; `togi-tool-sim` 18 crates, 16.49 s.
- **CI.** Job durations are `completedAt` minus `startedAt` from `gh run view <id> --json jobs`; cargo times are the `Finished` lines of the job logs.

  | job | #867, run 38263442579 on `03e32522` | #861, run 38260500290 on `dbbded33` |
  |---|---:|---:|
  | `rust-package` | 107 s | (none) |
  | `rust-sim-suite-0` | 268 s | 231 s |
  | `rust-sim-suite-1` | 259 s | 253 s |
  | `rust-sim-suite-2` | 268 s | 259 s |
  | `rust-sim-suite-3` | 262 s | 183 s |

  The most recent `main` push run before this pull request, 38261731768 on its base `bcf21d46`, took 23, 27, 29 and 41 s for the four shards and is no comparison: shard 0's log builds no derivation and fetches one path of 0.5 KiB, so those checks' outputs were already in the Cachix cache from #861's pull-request run. The table compares with #861's last pull-request run instead, which built them. In shard 0, the three dependency passes took 7.47 s, 11.93 s and 0.15 s for `togi-tool-sim-deps` there and 9.77 s, 14.52 s and 5.10 s for `togi-release-deps` here, and `togi-tool-sim` took 42.38 s there and 45.52 s here. In shards 1 to 3 of #867, `togi-release-deps` took 9.60 to 9.85 s, 14.41 to 14.57 s and 4.88 to 5.24 s; in the `rust-package` job 10.23 s, 14.67 s and 5.09 s, then `togi-rs` 11.64 s.

  The shared derivation saves work only within one Nix store. Each CI matrix job builds in its own store, and Cachix receives only each check's output closure, so each of the four shards and the `rust-package` job built `togi-release-deps` itself (each log has its own `building` line for the same derivation). The simulator shards now also build the extra dependencies above.
- **Not measured.** A cold local build of `togi-release-deps` (its cargo times are those of the log the daemon held), the run on a Mac, and agent cost.

## Stage 7: Rust bench baseline writer (#538, 2026-10-12)

- **Writer.** `togi-tool-bench --baseline-out FILE` writes the run's records as a baseline file keeps them, through `Record::baseline`, which drops `model_check` and `wall_s`; the projection lives in the crate and its tests, not in a recipe-side `jq` or `nu` filter like Go's `bench-baseline`. `just bench-baseline-rs OUT` runs it over `--split all`. `Record::wall_s` is now an `Option` that is left out when absent, so a baseline file reads back; every other field stays required and an unknown key, `partial_seconds` included, is still refused. Comparison and the gate read neither dropped field, so `--baseline` prints exactly the same against the slim file as against the full records. `--baseline-out` and `--out` may be given together, and `git::inspect` sets both aside when it decides whether the tree is dirty.
- **Path.** The recipe takes the output path as a required argument and does not touch `tools/bench/baseline.jsonl`. That file is still read as Go wrote it by the Go bench's `--baseline`, three Go tests (`TestCommittedBaselineIsSlim`, `TestCommittedBaselineIsRuleset11`, the suite-cleanup test), `togi-tool-bench sanity` and its default `--go`, two Rust tests that pin its 220 Go runs, `flake.nix` (the `rust-nextest` source fileset) and the research programs. A Rust recording there would break them and end the Rust-versus-Go sanity comparison; it switches in stage 9 ([#644](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/644)), which deletes the Go bench and those Go tests, after the cutover ([#643](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/643)) has compared the Rust suite with the Go scores the file holds.
- **Measured.** Release build, `bench --split all --no-cache --out FULL --baseline-out SLIM`, run directories in a temporary directory, no `--keep`: 220 records; full `--out` file 181,860 bytes, slim file 168,505 bytes (13,355 bytes, 7%, under the 200,000-byte target); 65.4 s of harness time (1 min 22 s wall with the build) on the development host. Rust's `model_check` is three counts, so it is far smaller than Go's 93%, and the full Rust file is already under 200 kB. `bench --split all --no-cache --baseline SLIM` against the same tree compared clean (`verdict: NEUTRAL`, 220 pairs, `equal=220`, V1 to V4 0) and printed byte for byte what `--baseline FULL` printed, the `harness_wall_s` line aside.
- **Tests.** `records`: `the_baseline_writer_leaves_out_the_model_check_and_the_wall_time_and_nothing_else`, `a_baseline_file_round_trips_through_the_reader`, `a_full_file_and_a_baseline_file_both_read_but_an_absent_wall_time_is_not_a_default`; `compare`: `a_baseline_file_compares_exactly_as_the_full_records_do`; `gate`: `a_baseline_file_is_judged_exactly_as_the_full_records_are`; `run`: `the_baseline_file_holds_the_runs_records_without_wall_times_and_compares_as_they_do`; `cli`: `the_baseline_out_file_is_a_baseline_the_command_accepts_and_prints_the_same_against`; `git`: `each_of_several_output_files_is_set_aside`. The Go tests of `tools/bench/baseline_compat_test.go` map as follows. `TestComparisonIgnoresProjectedFields` maps to `compare::a_baseline_file_compares_exactly_as_the_full_records_do` and `gate::a_baseline_file_is_judged_exactly_as_the_full_records_are`. `TestReadResultsIgnoresRetiredFields` is inverted on purpose: the Rust format has no `partial_seconds` and refuses it as an unknown key, which the existing `records::a_record_is_an_object_with_exactly_the_fields_the_format_names` pins. `TestCommittedBaselineIsSlim` stays Go: it pins the committed Go file, which this change does not touch, and it goes with the Go bench in stage 9.
- **Not changed.** The Go `bench-baseline` recipe, the committed baseline and the changelog: a development tool, no change a user of togi would notice.

## Stage 7: bench children keep temporary journals (#613, #868, 2026-10-12)

[ADR 0060](adr/0060-bench-children-keep-temporary-journals.md) records that bench children keep running with a temporary `--state-dir` and the parent reads and parses the journal, instead of the in-memory children reporting a digest that [#613](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/613) decided; `same`, `smoke` and `audit` will do the same once ported. The Rust children's dominant cost was the per-event `state.json` publication, found in [#868](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/868) and removed by [#871](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/871); the ADR carries the measurements and narrows, for bench children, the rule of the open ADR issue [#611](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/611).

## Stage 7: `tools/audit` (#824, 2026-10-12)

`togi-tool-audit` gains the sweep and a `clap` command, `togi-tool-audit`, beside its library ([#824](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/824)): 972 lines of library and command (`sweep.rs`, `report.rs`, `main.rs`, `interrupt.rs`) and 1,078 of tests (`tests/command.rs`, `tests/audit/sweep.rs`), counted with `wc -l`. No Go code is removed; `just audit-rs` runs the Rust sweep beside the Go `just audit`.

- **Design.** One crate with a binary, like `togi-tool-bench` and `togi-tool-sim`, since the command is a thin shell over the library's `audit_directories` and `Sweep`. The audit does not link `togi-tool-bench`: it runs the bench as a subprocess (the `togi-tool-bench` beside its own executable, or `--bench FILE`) and reads only the five members of each run record it needs (`scenario`, `seed`, `split`, `status`, `wall_s`; the record is object-only, so a positional array is refused), so the bench's record and the audit's library stay independent and `togi-tool-sim`, which uses the library, does not pull the bench in. The sweep writes the suite JSON with the gate, holdout and smoke seeds dropped and `dev` set to seeds 1 to N, with relative machine paths made absolute; the bench validates it. Without `--keep` the sweep runs in a temporary directory removed once it is audited, or when the bench fails ([#613](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/613): a default `audit-rs` leaves nothing behind; Go kept it and printed the path), pinned by `a_sweep_without_keep_leaves_nothing_behind`; a failure then says the evidence was removed and to rerun with `--keep DIR`. Neither Ctrl-C nor SIGTERM or SIGHUP leaves that directory or an orphaned bench (`interrupt.rs`): while the bench runs the audit forwards the signal to it and stops the sweep, and while it reads the evidence it removes the temporary directory and exits 1 with `interrupted`. The bench has its own watcher for the same signals, so a terminal's Ctrl-C reaching both is harmless. [#868](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/868) decided that the audit keeps #613's contract, in which kept sweep state goes to a temporary directory removed after use, not to persistent disk. The sweep keeps its children's journals on purpose, since it audits them; without `--keep` they go to a temporary directory under `$TMPDIR` that is removed after use, on a bench failure and on a signal. Whether that is persistent disk depends on `$TMPDIR`. A `censored` bench status is a `bench_status` finding and a bench run's live journal must conclude, as in Go, because the sweep checks that every dev session concludes within its boot cap. [#430](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/430) decided that a capped run is measured censored cost, not an error, and [#546](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/546), still open, owns auditing a capped run as an unfinished prefix, together with moving the checks into the bench; the port does not anticipate it. Bench output is copied to `bench.log` and stderr. The report is JSON Lines of `Finding` on stdout, with `check` one of the journal checks, `wall_time`, `bench_status` (not `termination`, which is a journal check) or `unreadable`.
- **Go bugs fixed.** One: Go's sweep reported a bench status other than `concluded` or `deadend` under check `termination`, the spelling of the journal termination check, so a consumer could not tell them apart; the port names it `bench_status`. Two behaviors differ on purpose: the audit takes each directory's writer lock, so a directory a tuner is running is refused rather than read while it changes, and the sweep no longer re-checks suite members, which the bench validates when it runs.
- **Tests.** `TestTimingChecks` maps to `timing_flags_a_run_over_ten_times_its_scenario_median` and `timing_names_the_run_directory_and_its_median`, its `termination` expectation for a run with status `error` becoming `bench_status`; `TestSweepSuite` and `TestSweepSuiteWire` to `the_sweep_suite_is_dev_only_over_seeds_one_to_n_with_absolute_machines` (an insta snapshot of the emitted JSON) and the fake-bench test that reads the suite the bench was given; `TestLoadSweepSuiteRejects` to `a_suite_that_cannot_be_swept_is_refused` (malformed, not an object, no scenarios) and, for its missing-suite case, `a_missing_suite_file_fails_before_running_the_bench`, the unknown-member and wrong-type cases moving to the bench's own suite tests; `TestRecordLookupIgnoresPathSpelling` to `a_directory_is_audited_with_the_run_it_belongs_to_whatever_its_spelling`, `a_retained_sweep_is_audited_again_from_its_records` and the dotted-path tests `a_directory_is_audited_with_its_run_whatever_dots_its_path_has` and `a_run_directory_is_found_whatever_dots_its_root_is_spelled_with`; `TestUsage` to `help_goes_to_stdout_and_a_bad_flag_exits_two`. New: a failed bench, a missing bench, an unreadable or missing directory, a run directory that must conclude while a given one need not, records that name no run (a positional array included), the spellings of the reported checks (`the_reported_check_spellings_are_pinned`), the `--sim` pass-through, the open error of missing run records, the `lock` file and mode 0711 an audit leaves in a directory, and a SIGINT, SIGTERM or SIGHUP while the bench runs (`real_process_a_signal_stops_the_bench_and_removes_the_scratch_without_blocking_its_signals`: the bench is gone, its signals were not blocked, the temporary directory is empty, a kept one stays) and while the audit reads the evidence (`real_process_a_signal_while_the_audit_reads_the_evidence_removes_the_scratch_and_exits`: a fake bench leaves a FIFO as the run records, so the audit blocks reading it deterministically).
- **Measured.** `cargo nextest run -p togi-tool-audit` on the development host (not a GitHub runner), dev profile, built tree: 67 tests in 0.44 s of wall time under nextest's parallel runner; the slowest are two of the four signal tests, `real_process_a_signal_before_the_bench_starts_stops_it_as_soon_as_it_does` at 0.35 s and `real_process_a_signal_while_the_audit_reads_the_evidence_removes_the_scratch_and_exits` at 0.25 s. Go's `tools/audit` tests were not timed again.
- **Regressions.** None known: no Go code changes, and the sweep is new beside Go's `just audit`.
- **Review findings fixed.** The first review found 2 P2 and 10 P3, all fixed. P2: nothing handled signals, so Ctrl-C left the temporary directory and SIGTERM or SIGHUP to the audit orphaned the bench; and the run record lookup used `std::path::absolute`, which keeps `..`, so a relative `--root` with `..` missed its record and the must-conclude check was skipped without a word. P3: a failure without `--keep` named an evidence directory already removed; the bench's log handle was cloned after it was spawned, so a failed clone orphaned the bench; the `--sim` pass-through, the missing suite file and the records-open failure were untested; the run record decoded a positional array; the audit's doc comment and these docs omitted the `lock` file and mode 0711 it leaves; the spellings of `wall_time` and `unreadable` were unpinned and the bench-status check was named `termination` like a journal check (now `bench_status`); and the line counts here were stale. The clone-after-spawn fix has no test: the clone cannot be made to fail while the spawn succeeds. The fix round found 1 P2 and 11 P3, all fixed. P2: during the audit phase the main thread's directory lock could recreate run directories the signal watcher was removing, so a Ctrl-C during the audit could still leave the temporary directory. P3: tests for a signal before the bench starts, an interrupted bench that still exits 0, the no-root error, an archived journal's `journal` name, a holdout run directory and the kept evidence path in a failed bench's message; the signal tests renamed `real_process_*` so nextest's real-process group limits them; and four docs fixes (the measurement wording, the Go `termination` collision as a Go bug, #868's rule for the audit's temporary directory, and #546's capped-run rule).
- **Sweep result.** `just audit-rs --seeds 20 --jobs 16` over the 11 scenarios of `tools/bench/suite.json` (220 dev runs on the Rust simulator, run directories on a RAM tmpfs, a 32-thread host also running other jobs) took 60 s wall, audited 220 journals in 220 directories and reported 0 violations; the job's cgroup peak was 2.9 GB, which counts the 1.8 GB of retained run directories held in tmpfs. An earlier run at `--jobs 8` took 169 s on a busier host with the same result. This is not a GitHub runner measurement.
- **Not measured.** The default 200 seeds per scenario (2,200 runs) was started and stopped before [#868](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/868) was fixed, when `togi-tool-sim` rewrote a `state.json` of about 50 KiB after every event, which for a retained 200-seed sweep is on the order of a terabyte of writes; the 20-seed sweep above ran on that simulator, and the 200-seed default has not been rerun since the fix. The run on a Mac.

## Stage 7: `cmd/togi` reset, restart-limit and doctor (#828, 2026-10-12)

The `togi` crate gains the three administration commands ([#828](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/828)): `reset --core N | --all`, `restart-limit --tuning-boot <grubenv>` and `doctor`, one module each (`reset.rs`, `restart_limit.rs`, `doctor.rs`), one variant and one arm in `cli.rs`.

- **Design calls.**
  - **The host seam.** `system::System` is the one way a command reaches the machine: platform, privileges, host lock path and ownership, boot id, clock, the hardware diagnosis, GRUB, the retry marker, the reboot, the default configuration file (`config_path`; `Real` answers `/etc/togi/config.json`, so no test reads the host's configuration, and `ConfigArgs::load(default)` takes it) and what colours stderr. `Real` is the host and `togi_cli::run` uses it; `togi_cli::run_with` takes any other, so a Linux test stands for another platform, holds the host lock on a temporary file or answers the diagnosis without touching hardware. `run` ([#829](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/829)) adds its own methods to the same trait. It is documented in the crate docs.
  - **Unsupported platforms** ([#642](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/642)). `doctor` asks `check_platform` before it reads the journal or the configuration, takes the host lock or makes a check: `togi doctor: hardware runs need Linux: unsupported operation`, exit 1. The test gives it a journal `run` would refuse (it would be 16), a held host lock (3), a missing explicit configuration (2) and a state directory that does not exist, and checks that the state directory is not created, the lock file does not appear and the diagnosis is never called. The spec's doctor order is changed to match (below). `reset` has no platform check: Darwin resets a copied journal under a private lock (spec, Host lock), and `System::host_lock` returns `Policy::Private` there.
  - **No hardware in tests.** `doctor` reaches `togi_hardware::diagnose` only through `System::diagnose`; the tests answer it with a `Diagnosis`, so every doctor exit code is reachable without root, a driver or a Zen CPU. The tests over the real binary stop before it: usage errors, a refused or unreadable journal and a bad configuration all exit before the lock and the diagnosis. The retry marker, GRUB and the reboot are fakes too, so no test writes `/run`, `grubenv` or reboots.
  - **A journal's build is read without a lock.** `doctor` judges the journal from its first line (`recorded_build`, the read-only twin of `StateDir::live_build`, one commit shared with [#826](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/826)), so a newer schema exits 16 even when its later events cannot be decoded, and an older one, which `run` would archive, has no session to compare. `reset --core` does the same under the writer lock before it opens the journal, since opening would decode and repair it.
  - **`reset --all` has three shapes**, chosen by the first line: a journal of another schema is archived unread (`archive_unreadable`); one of this schema, whatever its ruleset or epoch, gets `command.reset` and is archived by `togi_session::reset_all`; a state directory without a live journal drops a pending carry or finishes an interrupted incompatible archive (`StateDir::open` completes the pending move, `close` releases it) and says which. All three leave the permanent `-reset-all` marker. The check for "no journal" runs after the host lock and before the boot id and the state-directory lock, Go's order, and creates nothing in the state directory; the one exception, a pending marker in `archive/`, is found by listing the directory, so a state directory named like a glob pattern works.
  - `reset` takes the host lock, then the boot id, then the writer lock: a busy host lock and a held writer lock are both exit 3 and leave the session as it was. A core number is a `u8` to clap; a core in range that the session lacks is `CommandError::NoSuchCore`, exit 2.
  - `doctor` prints the table itself (columns two spaces apart, details escaped), and the `not ready` line on stderr is `Style::RedBold` through the renderer, so `NO_COLOR`, the terminal and journald decide as for the other commands.
  - **Errors are escaped in one place.** `report::fail` and `report::exit_with` write `togi <command>: <message>` through `escape_text`, so no command prints an untrusted path or error text raw.
- **Go bugs fixed.** `reset` and `doctor` ignored a failed write to stdout (`fmt.Fprintf` results dropped), so a closed stdout still exited 0 with the message lost; Rust exits 1 (`a_closed_stdout_is_an_error` for both). `doctor` made its journal and configuration checks before the platform check, so on another platform it read the journal and could exit 16 or 2 before saying it cannot run (the order is #642's decision, below).
- **Native changes.** `reset --core` appends one `command.reset` and no `shutdown` (journal.md, Reset commands; Go appended one). `--core -1` and `--core 300` are clap usage errors, not a custom message. A journal of a newer schema, ruleset or evidence epoch, or holding an event kind this build does not know, exits 16 from `reset` and `doctor` with Go's wording (`report::refusal_message`, `report::unknown_kind_message`, pinned verbatim by tests); the unknown kind comes from the journal's typed `ReadError::UnknownKind`, which `reset` and `doctor` match instead of Go's pre-scan of the kinds, and `reset --all` makes that check before it drops a pending carry. Go exited 1 for an unknown kind in `reset --core` and same-schema `reset --all` (`compat_test.go`, `unknown kind keeps exit 1`); Rust exits 16, since the journal is refused, not unreadable. Both texts name the build from the journal's first line (`session.start`), where Go named the latest build stamped by a `config.loaded` (`scanBuild`, `BuildOf`); the advice to install that build stays valid, since any build that resumed the session had its schema, ruleset and evidence epoch. Messages that come from a Rust library keep that library's text where Go's wording was the operating system's or Go's: a configuration that cannot be read (`cannot read config <path>: No such file or directory (os error 2)`), a host lock that cannot be opened, a reboot that cannot run (`systemctl reboot: run systemctl reboot: ...`), and the `(os error N)` suffix of an I/O error in a marker path (`create retry marker: open /run/togi-retry-tuning-boot: ...`). `reset` logs in the local time zone (jiff's `tz-system`; `TZ` is honoured, pinned by `system::tests::the_zone_follows_tz` with a POSIX rule); `tzdb-zoneinfo` makes `TZ` zone names resolve from the system zoneinfo directories, which no test checks, since the result depends on the host's zone files. A malformed first line (no `schema` stamp) is a decode error, exit 1, where Go read the missing stamp as schema 0 and exited 16. One workaround waits on an API: finishing an interrupted incompatible archive goes through `StateDir::open_at`, which creates an empty `events.jsonl`; `reset --all` removes it and syncs the state directory before it prints the archive line, and a failed removal or sync exits 1 naming the step and the path, with no archive line, until `togi-journal` can recover the pending archive without opening a journal.
- **Spec.** `docs/spec/runtime.md`: the `doctor` row (the platform check comes first, before the journal and the configuration), the Reset and compatibility section (`reset --core` appends no `shutdown`, as journal.md says; `reset --core` and same-schema `reset --all` refuse an unknown event kind with exit 16; `reset` and `doctor` name the build from the journal's `session.start`) and the Exit codes row for 16 (unknown event kinds); the stale statements to the contrary in runtime.md (Dashboard) and journal.md (rule 5) are removed.
- **Tests.** 79 tests added to the 48 the crate had (`cargo nextest list -p togi` on Linux lists 127: 40 unit, 23 over the binary, 21 for `doctor`, 37 for `reset`, 6 for `restart-limit`; the two real-binary `doctor` tests in `tests/cli` are Linux-only) and 7 snapshots (`doctor` ready, failed, context-changed and unprivileged, and the help of the three commands), with the 4 completion snapshots, the top-level help and the no-command text regenerated, reviewed against the spec and against the Go goldens (the four doctor tables are byte-identical to `doctor-*.golden`; the help texts are clap's). The `reset` and `doctor` tests install a journal from `togi-watchtest` scenarios into a temporary state directory (`togi_watchtest::install_events` installs a journal a test simulated itself) and run `togi_cli::run_with` against a fake `System`. The Go tests of the files in scope:

| Go test | Rust |
|---|---|
| `TestCommandsRefuseWhileLocked` | `reset::a_busy_host_lock_exits_3_naming_it_before_anything_changes`, `reset::a_held_state_directory_lock_exits_3_and_changes_nothing`, `doctor::a_busy_host_lock_exits_3_only_for_root`; the read-only half is [#826](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/826) |
| `TestResetRefusesHostLock` | the same `a_busy_host_lock_exits_3_naming_it_before_anything_changes`: exit 3, the lock path named, files and journal unchanged |
| `TestResetBelowProtectedSharedAncestorHonorsHostLock` | obsolete here: the lock path is `System::host_lock`, not a flag, and the ancestor rules are pinned by `togi-hostlock` (`directory_authorization_matrix` and the acquisition tests) |
| `TestResetAllCandidateSoloLimits` | `reset::reset_all_warns_for_each_solo_limit_at_or_past_its_failure_point` (at, deeper, shallower, the remedy text), `start_offsets_are_not_solo_limits`, `a_solo_limit_on_a_core_without_a_failure_point_does_not_warn`, `a_core_that_failed_at_zero_warns_with_the_remove_it_remedy` |
