  The tests of `hostlock_test.go` and `compat_test.go` that pin `run`, `status` and `events` map in #829 and #826.
- **Measurements** (host load 64 to 93 when measured, hyperfine 1.21.0, a private Go build cache in the worktree's `target/`, `GOFLAGS=-mod=mod`; CPU is user plus system, the mean of the runs, so the numbers are loose):

| Operation | Rust CPU [ms] | Rust wall [ms] | Go CPU [ms] | Go wall [ms] |
|:---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Build, `cargo clean -p togi` then `cargo build -p togi` (5 runs, dependencies built) against `go build ./cmd/togi` after appending a comment to `main.go` (5 runs) | 1737 | 813 ± 102 | 1009 | 445 ± 44 |
| Tests, `cargo nextest run -p togi` (102 tests when measured, built; 10 runs) against `go test -count=1 -tags integration -run <the mapped tests> ./cmd/togi` (133 test and subtest runs, the test binary cached; 10 runs) | 4648 | 2554 ± 3162 | 949 | 5846 ± 2878 |

  The Rust tests cost more CPU than Go's because nextest starts one process per test and each `reset` and `doctor` test simulates and installs a journal from `togi-watchtest` (about 0.3 s of the probe machine's session each), where Go read one checked-in `events.jsonl`; a committed journal was not taken, since it would age with the schema. The wall time of both is dominated by the load.
- **Not measured.** The time to compile the test binaries from clean, the Go package's whole test run, agent cost, and the run on a Mac (the crate is clippy-clean for `aarch64-apple-darwin` from Linux; not run on a Mac).

## Stage 7: `tools/bench` (c) (#823, 2026-10-12)

`togi-tool-bench same` and `forecast` port `--same`, `--smoke` and `--forecast` of `tools/bench` ([#823](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/823)); the gates came in [#822](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/822). `same BASE` compares a base tree with the current one: each is built with cargo and its simulator runs the suite's sessions, and the journals of the two must agree line by line once the build stamp is taken out. `--smoke` compares only the sessions the suite lists under `smoke`. `forecast` resumes the runs of the target scenario from a copy of a state directory with the real simulator and reports the tuner's forecast; `togi-tool-forecast` gives the record. `just same-rs`, `smoke-rs` and `forecast-rs` run them; the Go recipes are unchanged. Against its base, 140235101, at its head 109cf36c the pull request adds 2,609 lines of library to `crates/togi-tool-bench` (31 removed) and 2,874 of tests (none removed): `git diff --numstat 140235101dec690a6b92e49d292da51880c15213...HEAD -- crates/togi-tool-bench`, whose three-dot form leaves out the merges of `main`, gives +5,483 −31 over 19 files; of the added lines 2,716 are in `tests/`, `src/same/tests.rs` and the snapshots, and the 158 lines of the inline `build_tests` module at the end of `src/same.rs` are counted as tests too (2,609 + 2,874).

- **Own formats.** `same` and `forecast` are subcommands, so the flag combinations Go refused cannot be written (the two Go tests became usage-error tests). The digest cache is `togi/bench-rs/same/<key>` in format `togi-bench-same-rs-v1`, apart from Go's; its key is the simulator's hash and `--max-boots`, and a session is looked up by its identity, its replay flag and the digests of its machine files. The forecast record is `togi_tool_forecast::Record`; `files` lists a machine file before the files it extends.
- **Deliberate differences.** The build stamp is removed from the two stamped events (`session.start` and `config.loaded`) as a JSON value, the build description in `msg` included; every other line is compared byte for byte. The time report is the simulators' wall time, not CPU time, and is informational. The head builds in its own target directory and the base in one under the cache directory, after every base file is marked modified now: cargo takes a source file older than its last build for unchanged, so with one shared directory the second tree silently ran the first tree's binary (a test found it). That base target directory is locked across marking, building and copying the base simulator out of it, so concurrent comparisons that share a cache wait for each other: the waiting one prints a notice from the command, and an interrupt ends the wait. A run that fails or the first difference stops the simulators still running, as in `run`, and the failure reported is never an interrupted run it caused. An interrupt during the cargo builds is reported as interrupted (exit 1), never as equality. Run directories an abort interrupted are removed, `--keep` or not, since they hold only a partial run. A relative `--cache` is resolved against the bench's working directory, not the base tree cargo builds in. `forecast` takes its tree (commit and dirty flag) from the command, which reads git in its working directory, and names each machine, in the record's runs and in the model checks, by its path relative to the suite's directory whatever path the suite gives it; a machine outside that directory is refused.
- **Go bugs fixed.** None found in the ported code. The one bug met on the way, two trees sharing a cargo target directory, was in this port's first design and is pinned by `each_tree_is_built_and_its_own_simulator_run`.
- **Measured.** Release build, a cold cache, `--no-cache`, a base tree exported with `git archive` of the same commit, 32-thread host at load 87 to 100, at the earlier head bafecbcb and not re-run since: all 220 sessions of the committed suite per side (440 simulator runs) took 1,135 s wall (sessions 1,122 s, builds 13 s), 842 MB maximum resident set, and reported `0 of 220 sessions differ`. `--smoke` took 37 s for 11 sessions per side. At 109cf36c, on a built tree in the dev profile with the host at load about 56 on 32 threads, `cargo nextest run -p togi-tool-bench` ran 277 tests in 5.6 s (a second run; the first took 4.4 s). That is the whole crate, the run, gate and sanity tests of #821 and #822 included. Go's comparable part, `go test -count=1 -run 'Same|Forecast|CopyState|RelativeTo|EnsembleFiles|LoadRunsShares|NormalizeBuild|BuildComparison|FirstJournalDifference|CompareJournals|JournalDigest|SessionCache|CacheKey' ./tools/bench`, ran 37 tests (93 with their subtests) in 1.829 s (a second run; the first took 1.566 s). The two figures are indicative only: different test selections, one shared loaded host.
- **Regressions.** None known: the Go bench is unchanged. The pull request's edits to the crate's existing code are small: `Simulator::launch` split from the build, `build_in` running cargo under the fleet, `cache::touch`, `run::check_machine`, and `Fleet::stopping` made `pub(crate)`.
- **Review findings fixed.** The first review found 6 P2 and 10 P3. P2: the shared base target directory had no lock (two comparisons could run each other's simulator); Go's `TestSessionCache` was unmapped and the digest cache's key and input guards unpinned; the fail-fast race test did not reach its unreported-difference branch; the in-flight forecast fixture resumed from uncut history; `TestForecastRejectsDeadEndBeforeAnchor` was unported with a false reason; absolute machine paths could reach forecast names and records. P3: an interrupt during the builds could end in equality and exit 0; a relative `--cache` put the base target in the base tree; the forecast's model checks and its kept-runs message were untested; no command test of `same`'s failure arm; a #613 test checked only the returned value; stale rustdoc, a false `--keep` claim and stale log measurements. CI's nextest check also failed because the forecast read git from the working directory, which the sandbox lacks (the command now passes the tree). The fix rounds found 7 more P3: a lock wait no interrupt could end, an unpinned post-build interrupt check, the `--cache` help, failure-path leftover tests that could pass vacuously, an untested relative-suite naming case, the lock-wait notice printed from the library instead of the command, and a fixture that failed under a symlinked temporary directory, as on macOS. All are fixed in the pull request.
- **Not measured.** Compile times against Go, agent cost, the same comparison across two commits whose simulators really differ (the tests use fake simulators and two small cargo trees), and the run on a Mac.

## Stage 7: `cmd/togi` `status`, `events` and `watch` (#826, 2026-10-12)

`togi status`, `togi events` and `togi watch` ([#826](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/826)). `status` and `events` read the journal without the lock and print it; `watch` runs `togi_watch::terminal` (`run` on a terminal, `one_frame` otherwise). One module each in `crates/togi/src`, with `read.rs` (the build check, the read and the torn-tail notice `status` and `events` share) and `table.rs` (word wrapping and aligned tables at 80 columns). The branch builds on #869's seam (on `main`) and uses it: `report::{chain, current_build, fail, refusal_message}` and `System` (`zone`, `stderr_facts`), so errors read `togi <command>: <message>` with the message escaped, and there is one build stamp, one refusal wording and one `recorded_build`. Nothing here needs `ConfigArgs`: none of the three reads the configuration.

- **What reads what.** `status` folds the events through `togi_watch::model` (queued resets, combinations, hunt facts, R7 status), the journal's own state (session, in flight, dead end, last decisions) and `togi_tuner::State::project` (phase, failure points, BIOS profile, deepening, exposure, Tctl peak), and scans the events for what no fold keeps (carried and between-trial notes, hunt and combination sequence numbers, unanswered too-cautious defects). `events` filters with `togi_journal::Filter`; its `--kind` value parser takes the names from `KindSelector::names()`, so all four completion scripts list them.
- **Additive API in crates this PR does not own.** In `togi-tuner`: `CheckingView::exposure` (the evidence's per-workload exposure; a `WorkloadId` already names its regime, so the table keeps Go's REGIME column) and `Projection::last_clean_cycle` (from `Stage::Concluded { last_cycle }`, set per event with no history scan, so `status` names the latest clean cycle, not the latest started one; `the_last_clean_cycle_follows_clean_cycles_not_the_latest_started_cycle`). `togi_journal::recorded_build{,_in}` and `ReadError::UnknownKind { line, kind }` came with #869 and are on `main`.
- **Deliberate differences from Go.** Three, each on the native-contract ground. (1) An event of a kind this build does not know is a read error (exit 1) for every filter, worded for a read-only command by `read::unknown_kind` (`line N: unknown event kind "x"; this build does not know it`, the same for `status`, `events` and one-frame `watch`; #869's `unknown_kind_message` says the build "cannot safely modify" the journal, which is the wording for the commands that write; pinned by `read::{status,events,watch}::an_event_of_an_unknown_kind_is_refused_in_read_only_words_by_*`): the Rust reader decodes every payload into a typed event (`togi-journal` `reader.rs`, `DecodeError::UnknownKind`), so no unknown-kind event exists to print unfiltered, and `docs/spec/runtime.md` (Reset and compatibility) already refuses a current-schema journal with unknown kinds. (2) A journal of an older schema is refused with #869's `refusal_message` (for an older one it names `togi run` and `togi reset --all`), like a newer one, still with exit 1 as `docs/spec/runtime.md` says for read-only commands; the messages now name the dimension (`uses schema 1`) instead of Go's `schema is N`: `docs/spec/journal.md` lists Inspect (`status`, `events`, `watch`) as refusing "a different schema", and the Rust journal never reads Go's. (3) `watch` takes its problem frame, error and exit from `togi_watch::terminal::one_frame`: off a terminal it prints one frame of `--width` by `--height` (default `ONE_FRAME_SIZE`, 240x67, both at least 1, else exit 2), a missing journal prints the frames' `NO SESSION YET` and exits 0 with empty stderr, and a journal that cannot be read or folded prints its problem frame, then `togi watch: <error>` (escaped) on stderr and exits 1. On a terminal it runs `terminal::run`, which owns SIGINT, SIGTERM and SIGHUP (tokio, no unsafe; the process exits when the dashboard ends, so the listeners that stay registered never matter) and ends with status 0. Everything else prints what Go printed: `QUEUED` is `reset` while a reset is queued, the checking activity line ends `, not full: <missing>` when the cycle's steps lack the full-cycle coverage (computed from `togi_config::FULL_CYCLE`, as the tuner does), and the activity line gives search precedence over hunt, phase 2 and checking, as `tuner.Project` does. `events --json` re-serializes the decoded event, which equals the journal line for every event the writer produces (tested). Times are printed in the system zone (`TZ` is honored).
- **Go bugs fixed.** None found in `status`, `events` or `watch`.
- **Goldens.** The Go goldens are insta snapshots over `togi-watchtest` scenarios (the simulated sessions of the dashboard goldens), one per scenario (`crates/togi/tests/read/snapshots`): `status` is `status_stopped`, `status-phase1` `status_search`, `status-phase2` `status_round`, `status-unconfirmed` `status_unconfirmed`, `status-dead-end` `status_deadend`, `status-hunt` `status_hunt`, `status-parked-member-probe-group` `status_member-probe`, `status-combination` `status_combination`, `status-partial-checking` `status_checking`; `status-fallback-combination` is `read::status::a_fallback_combination_names_the_hunt_it_covered_every_candidate_of`, a test over a journal built for the case; `help-status`, `help-events` and `help-watch` are snapshots of the real binary's `--help` (`status_help`, `events_help`, `watch_help`). The Go event goldens (`events-core`, `-trial`, `-exact-kinds`, `-kind-group`, `-kind-without-group`, `-no-filters`, `-time-window`, `-json`) are tests that pin the filter rule on a simulated journal: `filters_narrow_the_readable_lines` (the `trial`, `core`, `kinds` (exact kinds, one of them a kind outside any group), `repeated` and `group` cases, as snapshots), `a_time_window_is_inclusive_since_and_exclusive_until`, `json_prints_the_journal_lines_unchanged`, and, for the unfiltered `events`, `json_prints_the_journal_lines_unchanged`, which runs it with no filter at all, since their `events.jsonl` fixture is a Go journal this build refuses.
- **Test mapping** (Go test, then the Rust test; `read::` is `crates/togi/tests/read`).

| Go test | Rust |
|:---|:---|
| `TestStatus`, `TestStatusPhases` (all but its latest-clean-cycle case), `TestStatusCombinationAndOpenHunt`, `TestStatusExceptionalActivity` (all but its fallback case), `TestStatusHunt…` goldens | `read::status::scenarios_render_the_status` (a snapshot per scenario) |
| `TestStatusPhases` (latest clean cycle case) | `read::status::a_concluded_phase_names_the_latest_clean_cycle_not_the_running_cycle`, with `togi-tuner`'s `the_last_clean_cycle_follows_clean_cycles_not_the_latest_started_cycle` |
| `TestStatusExceptionalActivity` (fallback case, golden `status-fallback-combination`) | `read::status::a_fallback_combination_names_the_hunt_it_covered_every_candidate_of` |
| `TestStatusRecordOnlyPartial` | obsolete here: the Rust journal has no record-only trials, so the partial has no record-only state to show; `togi-watch`'s `help_glosses_no_record_only_trial` pins that the help glosses none |
| `TestStatusHuntShowsDecisiveFailureSignal` | `read::status::a_hunt_names_the_decisive_failure_signal`; the carried cases (a hunt opened by a carried trial or a carried idle failure) are `a_hunt_opened_by_a_carried_trial_names_the_carried_signal` and `a_hunt_opened_by_a_carried_idle_failure_names_the_carried_signal`, with `togi-watch`'s `a_hunt_caused_by_a_carried_trial` and `a_hunt_caused_by_a_carried_idle_failure` |
| `TestStatusR7SelfSufficiencyAndTopRequester` | the R7 table in every checking snapshot; the fold itself is pinned by `togi-tuner`'s `r7_status_*` tests |
| `TestStatusShowsUnresetDefectResetCommands` | `read::status::a_too_cautious_defect_lists_reset_commands_until_each_affected_core_is_reset`, `a_reset_of_every_core_removes_the_defect` |
| `TestBetweenTrialMCEReadOnlyViews`, `TestHumanProjectedFieldsEscapeControls` | `read::status::a_between_trial_mce_is_listed_as_evidence_and_escaped`, `read::events::a_between_trial_mce_is_listed_and_its_controls_are_escaped`, `read::watch::a_between_trial_mce_shows_in_the_frame`; the escape itself is `togi-render`'s tests |
| `TestStatusEscapesJournalMessages` | `read::status::controls_in_an_in_flight_message_are_escaped`, `controls_in_a_carried_session_message_are_escaped`, `controls_in_a_decision_message_are_escaped` (and the between-trial message above), `controls_in_bios_context_fields_are_escaped` |
| `TestWriteStatusWrapsWideBIOS` | `read::status::wide_bios_fields_wrap_by_terminal_cells_and_stay_whole` |
| `TestWriteStatusWrapsWideDecisionNotes`, `TestStatusWideTablesWrapWithoutDroppingValues`, `TestWrapLines` | `table::tests::wide_decision_notes_wrap_under_their_row_within_the_width`, `a_row_wider_than_the_width_wraps_without_dropping_a_value`, `text_wraps_at_the_width_and_continues_under_the_rest_prefix`, `wide_text_wraps_by_terminal_cells`, `a_wide_cell_pads_to_its_cells_not_its_characters` |
| `TestWriteStatusPreservesUnicodeWords` | `table::tests::unicode_words_are_kept_whole`, `a_wide_word_that_exactly_fills_the_room_stays_on_the_first_line`, `a_combining_word_that_exactly_fills_the_room_stays_on_the_first_line` |
| `TestStatusFitsEightyColumns` | `read::status::status_fits_eighty_columns` |

| `TestCarriedEventsLeaveStatusUnchanged` | `read::status::carried_facts_add_self_sufficiency_evidence_but_no_live_regime_exposure`: changed, not byte-identical. - **Measurements** (measured before `watch`, the merge of #869 and the review-fix round; host load about 120 at the time, so CPU time is the figure; `cargo build -p togi` after touching `lib.rs`, dependencies built, 3 runs; `go build` after appending a comment to `main.go`; the Rust tests are the whole `cargo nextest run -p togi` of that time, the Go tests only the filtered `-run` set below, so the two columns cover different sets):

| Operation | Rust CPU [s] | Rust wall [s] | Go CPU [s] | Go wall [s] |
|:---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Build the binary | 3.3 | 1.0 | 0.8 | 0.3 |
| Tests: `cargo nextest run -p togi` (79 tests, built) against `go test -count=1 -tags integration -run <status, events, watch, compat and text tests> ./cmd/togi` | 15.6 | 4.3 | 9.3 | 1.8 |

  The Rust tests simulate the scenario sessions once per test binary process (the simulator of `togi-watchtest`); Go reads checked-in fixtures, which is most of the difference.
- **Review findings fixed.** The first review found 49 findings from 16 reviewers (17 P2, 32 P3; several reviewers reported the same defect), and the Go-test mapping audit added one P2, the unmapped `TestReadOnlyCommandsDoNotTakeHostLock`: 18 P2 and 32 P3, all fixed except the two rejections below. P2: `events --kind` completed only a whole value in bash, zsh and fish, and its completion was pinned by re-filtering clap's values, not the scripts; `status` counted characters, not terminal cells, so wide text overflowed 80 columns, and hunt GROUP columns misaligned once a padded row passed 80; a hunt started by a carried failure named no signal; a located hunt did not name its held loaded cores as the parked source, and the cycle-end source and a phase 2 round with completed checks were unpinned; `--config` rejection for the three commands, the host-lock rule and the unknown-kind escape (Rust Debug form, not the spec's) were unmapped or unpinned; the fallback-combination and record-only cases were mapped to tests that do not pin them; the changed-line coverage the issue's Acceptance asks for is in the pull request body. P3: the seam list's `unknown_kind_message`, the heading without `watch`, `events extra` and repeated `--kind` unpinned, the Measurements scope, `events` value flags completing file names, and the missing #44 and #5 rows. Rejected: a broken pipe on `events | head` stays exit 1, since a failed stdout write is an error for every command; the empty REGIME table header stays (owner decision). The fix-round review found 1 P2 (the stdout-write test opened Linux-only `/dev/full` on every platform; it is now Linux-only) and 1 P3 (the host-lock test did not prove the session files unchanged), both fixed, and the coordinator added 1 P3 (#432: `status` kept its own copy of the group and candidate words), fixed.
- **Not measured.** The run on a Mac (the crate is clippy-clean for `aarch64-apple-darwin` from Linux), agent cost, and a clean-build compile time.

## Stage 5: test mapping (#876, 2026-10-12)

[`rust-migration-stage-5-tests.md`](rust-migration-stage-5-tests.md) maps each of the 220 Go test functions of stage 5's eight packages (`internal/hostlock`, `smu`, `detect`, `backend`, `backend/mprime`, `backend/ycruncher`, `trial` and `hardware`, including the `integration` and `hardware` tagged files) to the Rust tests that pin the same behavior: 213 are pinned by a test of the port's own crate, 3 by another crate's and 4 are obsolete under the native contract (each with its reason). `togi-command` has no Go package and no rows. Every Rust name resolves to a test that `cargo nextest list --workspace` (or, for the `hardware` feature tests, `--features hardware`) reports at `34c29cd4`.

- **Tests added.** The mapping found behaviors a Go test asserted that no Rust test pinned, so this pull request adds 28 tests and changes no library code: three to `togi-detect` (a failed read keeps its error kind and command cause; every timed-out seam call keeps the timeout as its cause; a failed open of a matching pstore dump keeps its I/O cause), one to `togi-hardware` (a supported CPU with no driver loaded skips every gated check), one to `togi-session` (an escaped worker ends the trial inconclusive and dead-ends on containment, with no write after `trial.end`) and 23 to `togi-trial`, among them sweep timing under a hung signal or listing, a quiet instance's unterminated final error drained after a watched poll used the rest of the duration, symlink swaps of retained logs and reused trial directories, `/proc` stat parsing bounds, a FIFO replacing a watched file, the Tctl maximum, and the real-manager scope probe of the `hardware` feature, which the `vm-trial-scope-rs` check runs in both of its passes.
- **Not found.** No Rust bug.

## Stage 6: `tools/replay` (#837, 2026-10-12)

`togi-tool-replay` ([#837](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/837), part of [#641](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/641)) plays a recorded journal through the dashboard on a fast-forward clock, or prints one frame of it with `--at`. It ports `tools/replay/main.go` (198 lines) and its tests (386 lines): 652 lines of library, binary and inline tests, 1,140 of tests and 3 insta snapshots. `just replay-rs` runs it beside the Go `just replay`, which stays until the cutover.

- **Shape.** `playback` holds the clock rules as pure functions over `Event`: the gap to the next event is the monotonic difference when both events carry a stamp and the same non-empty boot, else the non-negative wall difference; scaled durations saturate; the speed is a `Speed` that can only be finite and positive, so clap rejects a bad one while the arguments are read, before any journal is. `frame` folds the prefix of events into a `Model` and draws it with `frames::render`; `live` feeds the playback to the loop; `load` reads the journal without a lock and refuses another ruleset or a newer schema once the first event is read, before any later event is decoded (the journal spec's Replay row).
- **The loop is one loop.** `togi-watch` had no way to run the dashboard on a journal that is not a directory on disk, and its clock was `Timestamp::now`. `terminal::run_with` now takes a `Feed` (the journal for a frame, and a reload) and a clock, and a `Config` that says which directory to watch (none for a feed that changes by itself), a redraw interval that holds whatever the frame says, and what ends the loop; `run` and `run_after` are wrappers over it, so there is still one loop, with the same signal contract. Replay's feed folds only when the shown prefix changes, its clock is the playback's display clock, and the 250 ms interval keeps playing through a frozen (R6) frame without the frames knowing about it. The change is additive, a source and a clock seam: the live `watch` contract of `docs/spec/runtime.md` (Dashboard: redraws, the R6 quiet hold, signals) is left unchanged.
- **Go bugs fixed.** The single frame was drawn with the key footer on while `togi watch`'s own one-frame print draws none; it is now drawn as that print is. `p.at += p.gap` could wrap when two saturated gaps were summed; it saturates. `--speed` was checked on every path, but `--view` only with `--at`, and `--width` and `--height` never; all are checked when the arguments are read.
- **Differences that are not bugs.** `--at 0` prints the first event's frame (Go played the recording, treating 0 as unset); `--after`, `--width`, `--height`, `--color` and `--view` need `--at` and `--from` conflicts with it, where Go ignored them; `--after` is read as `40s` or `1m30s` with jiff's duration syntax; `--color` writes the 16-colour sequences of the console profile; the events are folded once as the prefix grows, where Go projected the whole prefix again on every change; the journal's monotonic stamp is an `Option`, so Go's scan of the raw JSON for a top-level `mono_ms` is gone; a journal that cannot be folded is an error naming its event, not a frame.
- **Not read.** The Go-schema recordings under `tools/replay/testdata` and `internal/carry/testdata` are not read. The tests that played them (`TestRunFrameCurrentRulesetAllSchemas`, `TestRunFrameShippedSchemas`) map to the refusal tests and to frames of the `togi-watchtest` scenarios.
- **Spec.** None changed: the seam adds a replay source and clock and leaves the live `watch` behaviour the spec describes as it was.
- **Tests.** Every Go test of `tools/replay` maps to a Rust test or a stated reason in the pull request. The 50 tests of the crate take about 7 s under `cargo nextest run -p togi-tool-replay` and include two that play the built binary on a pseudo-terminal. A fake feed and clock drive the one loop in `togi-watch`. `just cover-rs origin/main` lists four uncovered changed lines: the let-else panic arm of a test helper, the `watcher.arm()?` of the notifier setup that only a polling (non-Linux) host takes and the line closing its task (both moved, not new), and the watchdog closure of a loop test that only runs on a hang.
- **Review findings fixed.** The review found one more P2 and two documentation defects. P2: the live feed in `live.rs` sampled the shown frame's event prefix and its timestamp independently, so a frame could pair a prefix with a time from another moment; the live writer now caches one coherent prefix-and-time frame, and a deterministic boundary test pins it. Documentation: this entry said `docs/spec/runtime.md` does not specify the live loop, but its Dashboard section does, so the sentence now says the change is additive and leaves that contract unchanged; and a leftover literal merge marker from an earlier merge was removed from this file.
- **Not measured.** Compile and test times against Go's, agent cost, and the run on a Mac.

## Stage 7: `cmd/togi` run, dashboard and panics (#829, 2026-10-12)

The `togi` crate gains `togi run` ([#829](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/829), the last of the four `cmd/togi` pull requests of [#642](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/642)): `run.rs` and its `outcome` and `prompt` modules, `signals.rs`, `panics.rs` and `dashboard.rs`. `togi-session` hands a panic back (`Ended.panic`, `Panic`, `SessionError::Panicked`) and `togi-journal` names an unknown kind (`ReadError::UnknownKind`).

- **Design calls.**
  - **The order of a run** is [#642](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/642)'s, platform first: the platform check (exit 1 before the state directory exists, the boot id, the journal or the host lock), then what reading alone decides, without a lock: the build verdict (a refusal appends nothing and exits 16; in the tuning boot it takes the host lock first, writes the leave reason and clears the saved entry) and the configuration (2); then the process's signal listener, the host lock (busy: 3), the journal's writer lock (`StateDir::lock`, held: 3), the verdict again under both locks, the boot id, the hardware, the carry, then the session. The host lock comes before the journal's lock, as in `reset` ([#80](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/80), [#99](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/99)), so the two commands cannot each hold the lock the other waits for; the first reading is not locked because the `togi` binary tests that run as an ordinary user (a bad configuration exits 2, a newer journal exits 16) could not get past a host lock. `the_host_lock_is_taken_before_the_boot_id_is_read_and_the_hardware_opened` pins the order against the `System` seam and `the_host_lock_is_taken_before_the_journals_lock_as_reset_does` pins the two locks (a busy host lock leaves the state directory uncreated; a busy journal lock is reached only after the host lock).
  - **The run owner is the session.** `togi_session::run` owns the cleanup of every exit once the host lock and the hardware are held. The binary prints the outcome and maps it to a status (`run::outcome`), closes the journal, drops the host lock and, only then, reboots when the tuning boot asked for it.
  - **Signals.** One process-lifetime thread (`togi-signals`, a current-thread tokio runtime) turns SIGINT, SIGTERM and SIGHUP into the cancellation of every registered run. The dashboard never puts the input in raw mode (`keys: None`), so Ctrl-C stays a signal.
  - **Quiet input.** While the dashboard shows, `QuietInput` clears only `ECHO` and `ICANON`; `ISIG` stays. Giving the input back restores the flags with `TCSAFLUSH`, which drops what was typed during the run, and the defect prompt flushes with `TCIFLUSH` (`discard_typed_ahead`).
  - **The dashboard is drawn by `togi-watch`** on its own thread behind the `Surface` seam; its failure or panic is contained (`panics::contained`), `run` says `togi run: dashboard: <message>; printing events instead` and the session goes on (#398). Event lines are dropped only while it shows (`Gate`), and what the session reports while it shows is printed after it stops. The `Gate` records the sequence of each closing event (`profile.restored`, `shutdown`) it kept off the screen, and at the end `print_hidden` prints exactly those, once each, in journal order; a closing line the dashboard let through as a live event line is not printed again. After a fallback between the restoration and the shutdown, the live `shutdown` line can therefore come before the hidden `profile.restored` line.
  - **Panics.** `panics::install` sets the hook. A panic on the owner thread runs the owner's cleanup (cancel, joined teardown, the offsets restored through the Smu seam, `ProfileRestored` recorded), then the dashboard stops, what the cleanup left (a dead end, errors of its own) is reported, the journal closes, the host lock drops, and the process exits 2 with `panic: <message> (location)`; a dead end that asked for a reboot does not reboot after a panic and says so. A panic before the host lock exits 2 with no lock and no events. A panic on any other thread or tokio task never ends the process: the hook runs before the unwind, so exiting there would also kill the panics `togi-trial` contains (the classifier, supervision) and the dashboard's, mid-trial, before anything was restored. The hook only prints the line (held back while the dashboard owns the screen), the thread unwinds, and the owner sees the loss as a closed channel and ends the run through its own cleanup. The signal listener, whose loss nothing would notice, cancels every run when it dies. The panic tests re-execute the test binary with a scenario in the environment and wrap the `Machine` through `Fake::wrap`; there is no fault-injection feature in the binary.
  - **The `System` seam** is #828's (`system.rs`, `report.rs`, `ConfigArgs`, merged in [#869](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/869)); `run` adds `open_machine`, `interactive`, `stderr` and `surface`. `ConfigArgs::load` delegates to the new `ConfigArgs::load_with_source`, which also says whether the configuration file was read, so `run` journals `config.loaded` with `file` true only for a file it read. Every test of `run` runs on `Fake` in this process; the signal, pseudo-terminal and panic tests send their own process a signal or re-execute it, so they need nextest (a process per test).
  - **Sync only for `run` and `reset`.** `run` locks its state directory with `StateDir::lock` (fsync per event). `StateDir::lock_unsynced` is a `disallowed-methods` entry of `clippy.toml` that is allowed only at `#[expect]` sites: in `togi-simrun` and `togi-watchtest` sources and in the tests of `togi-journal` and `togi-simrun`; a use in the `togi` crate fails `just lint`; no further guard was added.
  - **Platform targets.** `togi` has no `cfg(target_os)` in its sources; the command surface, the help and the completions are the same on every target (`clippy --target aarch64-apple-darwin -p togi` is clean, and the run help and the completions are snapshots). Only `togi-hostlock::DEFAULT_PATH` differs per target, and `run` asks `System::host_lock` for it. The tests that need Linux (`doctor` and `run` past the platform check, `/dev/full`) are gated in the test files; `run` exits 1 with `hardware runs need Linux` on another target before it reads anything.
- **Go bugs fixed.**
  - After a dashboard fell back to event lines, Go printed the closing `profile.restored` and `shutdown` lines a second time (`printCleanStop` scanned the journal and ran whenever a dashboard had existed); Rust prints only the closing events the dashboard hid, each once.
  - Go read the journal before the platform check in `run`, as in `doctor`; the platform check is first now (runtime.md changed).
  - No Go bug is claimed for a session panic: `TestRunOwnerEveryExit` has a panic mode, and Go's defers already joined the workload, restored the offsets, closed the journal and released the host lock before the process died. Rust keeps that cleanup.
- **Native changes.** A panic ends with one `panic: <message> (location)` line and exit 2 where Go re-panicked, and the session hands it back (`Ended.panic`) instead of resuming it. A panic that unwinds out of `togi-session`'s trial, appender or stop drain is now cleaned up as well: a workload that started is joined whatever panics after `trial::start`, a panic while an event is absorbed latches the appender (the emergency, no `ProfileRestored` from stale folds), a panic in the signal-stop drain still restores with readback, and a teardown whose stop cannot be confirmed is reported next to `Panicked`. `--cycles` takes `allow_negative_numbers`, so `-1` reaches the "must be a positive integer" message instead of being read as a flag. An unknown event kind is `ReadError::UnknownKind { line, kind }` (message `line N: unknown event kind "x"`), not a decode error, so `run` can name the kind: a current journal with one exits 16 before the configuration, an older one is archived (the configuration is read first and can still exit 2). The owner's decision on [#877](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/877) (strict decoding against additive changes): `run` and `reset` stay strict on unknown kinds (exit 16); the read-only commands become lenient in their own pull request, where `config.loaded` also gets a journal-owned type; state directories written by Go are [#886](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/886), a later pull request. The top-level help lists the `run --cycles 1` example from the same constant as `run --help` and no `status` example. [#869](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/869) is merged, so the `System` seam and `ConfigArgs` are on `main`.
- **Spec.** `docs/spec/runtime.md`: the Run recovery line (platform first) and the order of locks (host lock before the journal's), the dashboard paragraph (quiet input, the panic fallback, not reprinted), the panic paragraph (cleanup errors and a dead end reported, no reboot after a panic, a panic on another thread never ends the process, the consequence per thread) and exit row 2; `docs/spec/workloads.md`: the panic hook leaves the classifier and supervision catch of `togi-trial` alone.
- **Go test mapping.**

| Go test | Rust |
|---|---|

The closing lines are no longer found by scanning the journal for this run's suffix: the `Gate` records which closing events it kept off the screen, and `dashboard::tests::the_gate_says_for_each_event_line_whether_it_kept_it_off_the_screen` pins its decisions; a previous run's lines, a bounded suffix, restore without shutdown and the reverse, warnings only and an empty list follow from the set |

- **Decided Go issues.**

| Issue | Status |
|---|---|
| #11, #14 (SIGHUP, SIGINT and SIGTERM stop the run cleanly) | held, tested: `sigint_stops_a_run_cleanly_…`, `sighup_and_sigterm_stop_a_run_cleanly_like_sigint`, the pseudo-terminal Ctrl-C tests. The trial scopes of #14 belong to `togi-trial` |
| #80, #99, #170, #210, #213, #245 (the host lock first and held through cleanup; contention exits 3 before hardware or journal changes; the writer lock held through carry preparation) | held, tested: the host lock comes before the journal's lock, as in `reset` (`the_host_lock_is_taken_before_the_journals_lock_as_reset_does`), and before the boot id and the hardware (`the_host_lock_is_taken_before_the_boot_id_…`); `a_busy_host_lock_stops_the_run_before_hardware_and_any_event`, `a_busy_journal_lock_is_exit_three`, `a_tuning_boot_refusal_does_not_touch_the_boot_entry_without_the_host_lock` and `…_when_the_host_lock_is_not_safe`. The build verdict and the configuration are read once before any lock (they write nothing; the tuning boot's refusal takes the host lock first) and the verdict is judged again under both locks. The retry marker of #213 is `restart-limit`'s ([#828](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/828)) |
| #83, #176 (a panic restores, then propagates) | held, tested, fixed differently: the session catches the panic, cleans up (the workload joined, the offsets restored with readback, or the emergency) and hands it back, `run` reports what the cleanup left, closes the journal and drops the lock, then exits 2 with `panic:` instead of re-panicking; no reboot after a panic (`panics::*` and the `togi-session` tests above) |
| #194 (the tuning boot waits for an armed watchdog; a manual run does not) | held, tested: `the_tuning_boot_waits_for_the_watchdog_and_a_manual_run_does_not` (the session receives the bootloader only with `--tuning-boot`) |
| #212 (escaped output) | held for `run` and its dashboard fallback: every line `run.rs` writes with error or panic text (the diagnostic through `report::fail` and `exit_with`, `refuse`, the closing of the journal, `systemctl reboot`, the GRUB leave reason and clear) and `dashboard.rs`'s fallback line, the `panic:` line and the hook's `togi: panic on thread` line go through `escape_text`; the dashboard's frames are `togi-watch`'s. Tested with a control character: the owner's `panic:` line (`panics::a_panic_before_the_host_lock_exits_two_with_a_panic_line`), the hook's thread line (`panics::tests::a_panic_on_another_thread_neither_ends_the_process_nor_prints_over_the_dashboard`) and the dashboard's fallback line (`a_panic_in_the_dashboard_falls_back_to_event_lines_while_the_run_goes_on`). The text of the `systemctl reboot` error is pinned (`a_tuning_boot_dead_end_whose_reboot_fails_reports_it_and_keeps_the_dead_end_status`) without a control character. Code-reviewed only: the closing of the journal and the GRUB lines |
| #310 (the idle trial holds still) | not here: `togi-watch`'s loop |
| #311, #336 (restart-limit, count reset at the first durable append) | the subcommand is #828's; the reset and the leave reason are `togi-tuningboot` and the session; `run --tuning-boot` writes the reason before it clears the entry (the `the_refusal_…` tests) |
| #319 (stop and join the dashboard on every return) | held, tested: `a_failure_after_the_dashboard_showed_…`. Fixed by order: the journal is open before the dashboard shows, so a journal failure never shows it (`a_journal_that_cannot_be_opened_never_shows_the_dashboard`, `a_journal_that_is_not_a_journal_never_shows_the_dashboard`) |
| #364 (one command surface on both systems) | held: no `cfg(target_os)` in `togi`; `clippy --target aarch64-apple-darwin -p togi` clean; snapshots of the help and completions. Not run on a Mac |
| #367, #407 (the no-watchdog warning comes before the first write and the dashboard must not wipe it) | held, tested: the warning is a journaled `session.warning`, which the dashboard draws from the journal; the binary prints nothing of its own for it |
| #398 (a dashboard panic does not stop tuning) | held, tested: `dashboard::tests::a_panic_while_drawing_falls_back_to_event_lines_at_once_not_when_the_dashboard_is_hidden` and `panics::a_panic_in_the_dashboard_falls_back_to_event_lines_while_the_run_goes_on` |
| #593 (quiet input: ECHO and ICANON off, ISIG on, flush when it stops) | held, tested with descriptors of a pseudo-terminal: the three `dashboard::tests` above |

- **Tests.** `cargo nextest run -p togi`: 325 tests for the whole crate; the signal, pseudo-terminal and panic tests need nextest. Mutations checked by hand: giving the input back with `TCSANOW`, dropping SIGHUP from the listener and passing the bootloader to every run each fail the test that pins them. The review fixes were each checked against a test that fails without them where a revert was practical (`togi-session`'s join of a started workload, its latched appender, its drain catch and its report of an unconfirmed teardown; the prompt's wake-up on a signal and its read error as no); the prompt's partial-line read cannot fail by revert, since the old blocking read would hang the test instead. The journal-failure emergency test's child lifts its file-size limit after the failed append so the coverage profile it leaves is not cut short.
- **Measurements** (a loaded host, load 85 to 96; CPU is user plus system): building `togi` costs 2.44 s CPU (wall 1.15 s) against `go build ./cmd/togi` at 0.98 s (0.41 s); the 109 tests then in the crate cost 0.94 s CPU under nextest against the 97 mapped Go tests at 0.47 s.
- **Not measured.** A Mac, the compile time of the test binaries from clean and agent cost.

## Stage 6: closing measurements (#838, 2026-10-12)

Stage 6 ([#641](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/641)) ported `internal/watch`, `internal/watch/watchtest` and `tools/replay` in five pull requests: [#846](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/846) (the model, history and words of `togi-watch`), [#850](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/850) (`togi-watchtest`), [#853](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/853) (the terminal loop), [#863](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/863) (the frames, wired to the loop) and [#885](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/885) (the replay tool, `togi-tool-replay`, for [#837](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/837)). This entry closes it with the measurements #838 asks for. Each was measured on its own branch as it merged; the replay figures are those of the `tools/replay` entry above, merged with #885.

- **Test mapping.** [`rust-migration-stage-6-tests.md`](rust-migration-stage-6-tests.md) maps each of the 172 Go test and benchmark functions of the three packages (162 in `internal/watch`, none in `watchtest`, 10 in `tools/replay`) to the Rust tests that pin the same behavior: 169 are pinned by a Rust test or benchmark and 3 are obsolete under the native contract, each with its reason (`TestMain`, `TestProjectRecordCrashHistoryShrinksAfterRetention` and `TestRunFrameShippedSchemas`). No Go test is left without a Rust twin or a reason. `internal/watch` has no Fuzz or Example function and one Benchmark, `BenchmarkProject`, which `crates/togi-watch/benches/frames.rs` ports.
- **Decided Go issues.** [#536](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/536) and [#539](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/539), which the merged frames did not yet follow, are fixed in this pull request.
  - **#536 (fewer snapshots at three sizes).** A cut keeps its snapshots at 240x67, 160x45 and 120x33 only when it draws a size-dependent panel no other kept cut draws. Nine of the 18 cuts keep 240x67 only: `confirm`, `between`, `stopped` and `deadend`, which Go dropped, and the Rust-only `confirmation`, `concluded`, `unconfirmed`, `crashed` and `first_result`. 18 snapshots are removed (90 to 72 files), none of the cuts that keep three sizes changed, and `frames::snapshots::every_scenario_fits_the_frame_at_every_main_size` draws every cut at all three sizes through the bounds check without writing a snapshot. The reasoning for each cut is in a comment above `main_view_tests!` in `snapshots.rs`.
  - **#539 (help explains the words and links the tuner spec).** WHAT TOGI DOES says in one line each what the stages show, and ends with the plain-text link to `docs/spec/tuner.md`; WORDS keeps each definition, glyph, colour, counter and load label and drops the scheduling, evidence and threshold rule that followed it. One READING THE SCREEN note, which stated a condition, now says only that a core cannot go deeper. `help_links_the_tuner_spec_and_states_no_rule` checks 29 removed rule phrases where it checked five; review found the FOUND definition still ended `until every core has one`, a scheduling condition, so FOUND now reads `solo limit checked; waiting at 0` and that phrase is the 29th. The help snapshot diff touches the 8 `togi-watch` help snapshots and `togi-tool-replay`'s `help-80x24` snapshot, which repeated the old READING THE SCREEN note. `docs/spec/runtime.md` says the help explains the screen's terms, decides nothing and links the tuner specification.
- **Lines of code.** Counted on this pull request merged with `main` at #885's merge and before the review's FOUND help correction, over the files `git ls-files` lists per crate, as newline counts. Rust tests are `frame_tests.rs`, the files under a `tests/` or `frame_tests/` directory, every `tests.rs`, `testing.rs` and `snapshots.rs`, and in every other source file the lines from its first `#[cfg(test)]` line, indented or not, to its end; the rest of `src/` is library, and `benches/` is counted apart. Snapshots are `.snap` files, counted as files and not as lines. The Go count is `cat | wc -l` of a package's own non-test `.go` files and of its `_test.go` files, helpers such as `golden_test.go` included; goldens are the `.golden` files under `internal/watch`, counted as files, and Go's recorded gzip journals are counted neither as lines nor as goldens. The per-pull-request entries above split library and tests by their own rules, so their figures are not added to these.

  | Package | Rust library | Rust tests | Rust snapshots | Go library | Go tests | Go goldens |
  |:---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
  | `togi-watch` / `internal/watch` | 13,500 (+96 of benchmark) | 21,340 | 72 | 7,397 | 6,109 | 65 |
  | `togi-watchtest` / `internal/watch/watchtest` | 1,176 | 803 | 0 | 44 | 0 | 0 |
  | `togi-tool-replay` / `tools/replay` | 602 | 1,252 | 3 | 198 | 386 | 0 |

  `togi-watch`'s lines include the caller-owned feed and clock of the loop that #885 added and the words `status` shares with it. The replay entry's 652 and 1,140 lines are its own rule (everything outside a `tests/` directory as one figure, inline tests included) on its branch before the review fix; this rule moves the inline tests to the test column. Go's `watchtest` holds `doc.go` and `fixture.go`.
- **Tests.** On the merged tree `cargo nextest list --locked -p togi-watch -p togi-watchtest -p togi-tool-replay` lists 572 tests in `togi-watch`, 34 in `togi-watchtest` (its integration binary) and 51 in `togi-tool-replay` (45 in the library and 6 integration tests, 2 of which play the built binary on a pseudo-terminal), 657 in all. They were exercised in the full workspace gate below, which passed; the per-crate run counts that follow are historical: `cargo nextest run -p togi-watch` reported 570 tests on this pull request's frames fix before the merge of #885, and #885's entry counted 50 tests for `togi-tool-replay` before its review fix added a 51st, `a_frame_is_drawn_at_the_time_of_its_own_journal_even_when_an_event_falls_due_between_them`. #885 and #879 since added two `togi-watch` test functions (`a_caller_owned_feed_and_clock_drive_the_one_loop_with_no_directory_watched` and `the_group_and_candidate_words_are_shared_with_status`).
- **Suite time.** The per-crate timings are historical, each from the run named, none repeated for the crate alone on the merged tree. The final full-workspace figures are below.
  - `cargo nextest run -p togi-watch` took about 6.5 s for 569 tests when #863 was measured, on a development host that was running other builds; this pull request's 570th test (the bounds test) has no separate time. The terminal loop's 123 tests took about 2.1 s, 2 s of it one test waiting for two once-a-second frames, against about 0.11 s for the 28 ported Go tests under `go test -count=1 -run` for them (#853). The `togi-watchtest` suite took 2.5 to 5.2 s under nextest's parallelism, its slowest test 2.2 s run alone, on a 32-thread host at load 17 to 21 (#850), and #885 measured about 7 s for the 50 tests `togi-tool-replay` had before its review fix.
  - The whole workspace, 4,271 tests, passed in 55.8 s under `just cover-rs origin/main` on this pull request's frames fix before the merge of #885, which runs under `cargo llvm-cov` and on a host with sibling load; that is not a figure for the `togi-watch` suite. #885's review record reports 4,549 tests passed under `just cover-rs` on its first reviewed head and 4,643 after its fixes, with no time given.
  - Final gate, measured before #880 merged, on this pull request's merged tree at `f173cc04` (the source was the same at the #880 merge, before the review's FOUND help correction; the whole-workspace test count now differs): 4,674 of 4,674 workspace tests passed under `cargo nextest` with the CI profile in 21.173 s, and `just cover-rs origin/main` passed 4,674 of 4,674 default-profile instrumented tests in 25.103 s, printing "Every changed Rust line the trace measures ran in a test since origin/main." These are whole-workspace figures, not times of the stage's suites alone.
- **Frame time.** Criterion, `cargo bench -p togi-watch --bench frames`, from #863 on the development host while it ran other builds; Go's figures are from a scratch `RenderView` benchmark that was not committed, on the same host. A 240x67 frame drawn and encoded as the bytes a first draw writes, which is what Go's `RenderView` produced as styled strings, takes 0.60 ms for the longest scenario (`hunt-bios`, 11,230 events) and 0.54 ms for the first R7 checking trial; drawing into the buffer alone takes 0.27 and 0.32 ms; folding the checking journal takes 0.84 ms. Go took 1.17 ms for its `hunt-bios` journal (11,625 events, since the Go simulator's journal differs) and 2.05 to 2.31 ms for the checking trial, and `BenchmarkProject` took 4.1 to 6.3 ms. The frames fix of this pull request changes no drawing code, only the help text, and was not benchmarked again; nor was the loop after #885's feed and clock change.
- **Review findings.** Rows of the review records on each head, by priority and outcome:

  | PR | P0 | P1 | P2 | P3 | Fixed | Rejected |
  |:---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
  | #846 model | 0 | 0 | 4 | 16 | 20 | 0 |
  | #850 `togi-watchtest` | 0 | 0 | 1 | 6 | 7 | 0 |
  | #853 loop | 0 | 0 | 11 | 20 | 30 | 1 |
  | #863 frames | 0 | 0 | 10 | 28 | 38 | 0 |
  | #885 replay | 0 | 0 | 2 | 1 | 3 | 0 |
  | **total** | **0** | **0** | **28** | **71** | **98** | **1** |

  The #853 count covers its two records (a later head was reviewed again). #885's record has one: eight reviewers on the first head, the live and documentation reviewers again on their fixes; its P2s were the live feed sampling the shown prefix and its time apart and the entry's claim that `docs/spec/runtime.md` does not specify the loop, its P3 a leftover merge marker in this file.
- **Go bugs fixed in the stage.** The unrecovered crash's view had no start or partial flag (#846); the inotify climb toward a missing state directory could wait forever when a directory was made while it passed (#853); after a trial was over the CCD tables still marked cores under load, an idle failure that was not a crash read as one, a known idle failure printed an empty name, `tightNext` left a trailing space, "1 clean cycles" and the R7 top cell `no cores` for a workload with no top requester (#863); the replay tool's single frame carried the key footer, its playback time could wrap when two saturated gaps were summed, and `--view`, `--width` and `--height` were not checked when the arguments were read (#885). #536 and #539 above are Go issues the port had not followed.
- **CI failures.** The `check` workflow on each branch, counted from `gh run list`: #846 `rust-watch-model` 2 successes, 1 failure and 2 cancelled; #850 `rust-watchtest` 3 successes, 1 failure and 4 cancelled; #853 `rust-watch-loop` 4 successes, 1 failure and 2 cancelled; #863 `rust-watch-frames` 4 successes; #885 `rust-replay` 4 successes and 1 cancelled. A cancelled run is one a later push superseded. The three failures were in the `rust-nextest` check: on #846 and #850 `togi-session` `whole::crash::killing_the_run_at_every_durable_event_resumes_to_the_same_final_state` timed out at 120 s on the runner, a test of a crate this stage did not touch, and on #853 two `real_process` tests of `togi-watch` timed out at 30 s on the runner. The later runs of each branch succeeded; what changed between them was not looked into here.
- **Not measured.** Compile times against Go (`cargo build` and `nextest --no-run` against `go build` and `go test -c`), the Go package's whole `go test ./internal/watch` run next to the Rust suite's, isolated per-crate suite times of `togi-watch`, `togi-watchtest` and `togi-tool-replay` on the merged tree (only the whole-workspace gate was timed), agent cost by model, the run on a Mac (the crates are clippy-checked for `aarch64-apple-darwin` from Linux only; not run on a Mac) and the frame time on the Linux console.
- **Limits.** Frame times are single runs on a loaded development host and indicative only; the Go benchmark that they are set against is not committed. An append to the journal reads the whole file again and folds the new events (`Recording` has no read from an offset).

## Journal: lenient read-only decoding and a journal-owned configuration snapshot (#877, 2026-10-12)

[#877](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/877) asked two questions the read-only audit of decided Go fixes raised after #868: strict decoding against additive changes ([#123](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/123)) and `config.loaded` embedding `togi_config::Config` ([#125](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/125)). The owner's decision: `run` and `reset` stay strict, and an unknown kind or field refuses the journal (exit 16, naming it); `status`, `events`, `watch` and the tools skip unknown kinds, ignore unknown fields and say what they skipped; additive fields and kinds keep needing no schema bump; `config.loaded` gets a journal-owned snapshot with a wire golden. It lands before the cutover ([#643](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/643)), which starts a fresh journal, so there is no migration. No changelog fragment: the formats a user sees do not change.

- **Scope.** `togi-journal`: `Decoding::{Strict, Lenient}` over the one decoder; `EventReader::lenient`, `Recording::read_lenient` and `read_in_lenient`, `StateDir::recordings_lenient`; `Skipped`, whose `Display` is the one line the readers print (`skipped N events of kinds this build does not know (kind at lines …); ignored unknown fields: kind path (line …)`, escaped). A skipped event keeps its place: its envelope is checked as any other's, so its seq counts toward contiguity, a later event may cite it as a cause, and a lookup by seq finds nothing. A first event that is not `session.start` is never skipped. Strict decoding names an unknown field by its path (`data.cores[0].extra`, also in the members of internally tagged enums such as `smu.intent` and `trial.signal`'s schedule), where it named only the kind before, so `run`, `reset` and `doctor` refuse an unknown field as they refuse an unknown kind (exit 16). `togi`: `status`, `events` (and `--json`) read with `read::open` leniently and print the skipped report on stderr after their output; one-frame and live `watch` keep it in the footer after the key hints, shortened to a pointer to `togi events` when narrow; the footer is now drawn without key hints when it has something to say. The tools `togi-tool-audit`, `-facts`, `-stats` and `-trialfacts` and the forecast reader read leniently and print the report to stderr naming the journal; `togi-tool-replay` follows in its own slice. Help of `status`, `events`, `watch` and `reset` and `docs/spec/{journal,runtime}.md`, `docs/benchmarking.md` say it.
- **Snapshot, config and tuner API.** `togi_journal::ConfigSnapshot` (and `ConfigDurations`, `ConfigEvidence`, `ConfigChecking`, `ConfigDeadEnds`, `ConfigBackends`) owns the wire shape of `config.loaded.config`; the journal crate no longer depends on `togi-config` (the fuzz lockfile drops it). `Config::snapshot()` writes it and `Config::from_snapshot()` validates a recorded one as a configuration file is validated, returning `ConfigError`. Callers that took the recorded `Config` now convert: `TunerConfig::from_loaded` returns `Result` and a refused snapshot is the new `FoldError::Config` of the tuner fold; the simulator's resume reads `recorded` through `from_snapshot` and has a `Config` error variant. Decoding a snapshot checks only its own types (core ids, offsets, regimes, unsigned integers), not the configuration's policy ranges, so a journal stays readable when the configuration later narrows what it accepts. `a_fully_populated_config_loaded_matches_its_golden`, `a_minimal_config_loaded_matches_its_golden` and `the_snapshot_wire_keys_are_those_of_the_spec` pin the wire.
- **Seq holes in `State::apply`.** A lenient read leaves a hole in the seqs where it skipped an event, so `State::apply` no longer requires the next event to be `last + 1`: it requires `seq` greater than the last folded one (the first must still be seq 1). An event that does not come after the last is still `FoldError::Gap`. The readers still guarantee contiguity from 1 over the events they read, skipped ones included, so a hole in a fold means a skipped event, never a missing one; pinned by `an_event_that_does_not_come_after_the_last_is_a_fold_error_and_a_hole_is_not`.
- **Go decisions.** See the table below.
- **Tests added or changed** (`togi_journal::` unless noted). `tests::lenient::{an_unknown_kind_is_refused_by_strict_and_skipped_by_lenient, an_unknown_field_in_any_object_is_named_by_strict_and_ignored_by_lenient (property)}`, `reader::tests::{a_strict_reader_refuses_an_unknown_field_naming_its_line_kind_and_path, a_lenient_reader_skips_unknown_kinds_and_ignores_unknown_fields_and_says_so, a_lenient_recording_finds_events_by_seq_around_a_skipped_one, a_lenient_reader_still_checks_seq_causes_and_the_first_event_of_what_it_skips, the_skipped_report_is_one_line_naming_kinds_lines_and_fields, the_skipped_report_is_empty_singular_and_escaped_as_the_case_may_be, the_lenient_entry_points_read_files_and_directories}`, `tests::config_snapshot::*`; the fuzz target `reader` also reads leniently. `togi`: `read::commands::{the_read_only_commands_skip_an_unknown_kind_and_ignore_an_unknown_field_and_say_so, an_unknown_kind_that_is_hostile_is_escaped_in_what_the_read_only_commands_say, an_unknown_kind_as_the_first_event_is_never_skipped}` and `reset::{a_current_journal_with_an_unknown_kind_exits_16_and_is_left_alone, a_current_journal_with_an_unknown_field_exits_16_and_is_left_alone, an_unknown_kind_keeps_a_pending_carry_and_refuses_an_older_ruleset_too}`; `togi-watch`: `the_skipped_report_follows_the_keys_whole_and_then_as_a_pointer_that_keeps_them` and `an_event_of_an_unknown_kind_is_skipped_and_reported_in_the_snapshot`; the tools: the audit's `an_unknown_kind_in_the_middle_is_reported_and_leaves_no_violation`, facts' `an_event_of_an_unknown_kind_is_skipped_named_on_stderr_and_does_not_fail_the_command`, stats' `an_event_of_an_unknown_kind_is_skipped_and_named_on_stderr_and_the_report_is_still_written`, trialfacts' `an_event_of_an_unknown_kind_in_the_middle_is_skipped_and_reported` and forecast's `a_lenient_read_skips_an_event_of_an_unknown_kind_and_hashes_the_file_as_it_is`. The help snapshots of `status`, `events`, `watch` and `reset` changed with their text.
- **Corrections to earlier entries.** The claims below were true when written and are not now; the entries above stay as they were. The pilot's *Strict nested decoding* bullet and the `Wire` bullet say an unknown kind or field is a decode error: that is the strict reader, and the readers of the read-only commands and tools are lenient. The #826 entry's deliberate difference (1) says an event of an unknown kind is a read error (exit 1) for every filter, with the wording of `read::unknown_kind`, and that `docs/spec/runtime.md` was waiting for #877: `status`, `events` and `watch` now skip it and report it, and the spec says so; `read::unknown_kind` still words a refused first event or a one-frame `watch` read of a journal that fails otherwise. Its measurements and test counts were of that time and are not remeasured here.
- **Integration with `togi run` (#829) and the strict exceptions.** `run` words its refusal with `report::unknown_event_message` as `reset` and `doctor` do, so an unknown field is refused as an unknown kind is (exit 16, the path named, the journal untouched); pinned by `lifecycle::an_unknown_event_field_in_a_current_journal_is_refused_naming_its_path_and_writes_nothing`, beside the unknown-kind tests. The simulator (resume, history, the simulated journal) and `bench` stay strict on purpose: they replay, resume or append to the journals they read, so skipping an event they cannot decode would let them act on a journal they do not understand; `journal.md` lists them. The journal spec also says how a historical `tier.change` is read: [ADR 0028](adr/0028-remove-tiers.md)'s "opaque" describes the Go reader, and the native decoder skips and reports it as any unknown kind.
- **Go bugs fixed.** None found; Go's journal read, `events` and `watch` were not changed.
- **Not measured.** Compile and test times against Go's, the size of the change against Go's, agent cost, and the run on a Mac.

| Go issue | Status |
|:---|:---|
| #123: one registry; read-only commands tolerate an unknown kind; `run` and `reset` refuse it | held, with one difference the owner decided in #877: the registry is the `Payload` enum, so there is no second list; a payload that would not decode again is refused before it is written (`Writer`); `status`, `events`, `watch` and the tools skip an unknown kind (Go kept it as an opaque event and `events` still showed the line, Rust does not print it and says it skipped it); `run`, `reset` and `doctor` refuse it, now an unknown field too, exit 16, naming it and both build stamps; no schema bump per kind or field (ADR 0009) |
| #125: a journal-owned `ConfigSnapshot` | held: `ConfigSnapshot` with the current wire names, `Config::snapshot` and `from_snapshot` convert at the boundary, the journal crate imports no configuration crate, and two wire goldens pin it. The real journals Go pinned (#113) are not read: the cutover starts a fresh journal |
| #127: a skipped kind still counts in the seq order | held: `a_lenient_reader_still_checks_seq_causes_and_the_first_event_of_what_it_skips` (gap, late cause, first event) |
| #121: the fuzz target's invariants and corpus | the reader fuzz target now also drives the lenient reader; its corpus is unchanged |
| #132, #212, #535 | held as in the #826 and #869 entries: `--kind` is validated before the journal is read, the skipped report escapes hostile kinds (`an_unknown_kind_that_is_hostile_is_escaped_in_what_the_read_only_commands_say`), and the compatibility verdict is unchanged |

## Stage 7: closing measurements (#831, 2026-10-12)

Stage 7 ([#642](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/642)) ported the evaluation tools and the binary: `tools/modelcheck`, `tools/forecast`, `tools/facts`, `tools/fit`, `tools/stats`, `tools/bench`, the sweep of `tools/audit` and `cmd/togi`, and packaged the Rust binary. Sixteen pull requests carry it: [#841](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/841) (`togi-tool-modelcheck`), [#844](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/844) (`togi-tool-forecast`), [#832](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/832) (`togi-tool-facts`), [#852](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/852) (`togi-tool-fit`), [#851](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/851) (`togi-tool-stats`), [#857](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/857), [#862](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/862) and [#872](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/872) (`togi-tool-bench`: runs, metrics, comparison and cache; gate reports; `same`, `smoke` and `forecast`), [#866](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/866) (the bench refuses a simulator that is not a program, on every platform), [#870](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/870) (the `togi-tool-audit` sweep), [#861](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/861), [#869](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/869), [#879](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/879) and [#880](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/880) (`togi`: parsing, exits, version and completion; `reset`, `restart-limit` and `doctor`; `status`, `events` and `watch`; `run`, the dashboard and panics), [#867](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/867) (the Rust package) and [#878](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/878) (the slim bench baseline writer). Three more are counted apart below: [#860](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/860) (the dev profile at `opt-level = 1` for the whole workspace, [#859](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/859)), [#871](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/871) (the simulator publishes its state file only when a run stops, [#868](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/868)) and [#882](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/882) (ADR 0060). No Go code is removed or changed: the Go tools, recipes and package stay until the cutover ([#643](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/643)) and stage 9 ([#644](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/644)). This entry closes the stage with the measurements #642 asks for.

- **Test mapping.** [`rust-migration-stage-7-tests.md`](rust-migration-stage-7-tests.md) maps each of the 305 Go test functions of the eight packages (`tools/modelcheck`, `forecast`, `facts`, `fit`, `stats`, `bench`, `audit` and `cmd/togi`, with the `integration` tag) to the Rust tests that pin the same behavior: 289 are pinned, 9 are obsolete under the native contract (each with its reason) and 7 are the stage-4 rows of `tools/audit`, repeated there with their status; none is pending. The inventory of Go tests and the measurements below are of `d1a4dd63`, `main` after #880 (the measurements in two cohorts, see Host and method); the mapping's Rust names were re-resolved after #899 (`dfa2ee21`, the lenient read-only decoding of #877) was merged into this branch, and every one resolves to a test that `cargo nextest list --workspace` reports on the branch. #899 removed three tests that pinned the refusal of an unknown kind in read-only words (`status`, `events` and `watch`, and the hostile-kind escape on stderr); the rows of `TestCommandsWithFutureKind`, `TestEventsUnfilteredUnknownKind` and `TestHumanJournalReadErrorEscapesUnknownKind` now cite the tests of the lenient behavior (`the_read_only_commands_skip_an_unknown_kind_and_ignore_an_unknown_field_and_say_so`, `an_unknown_kind_that_is_hostile_is_escaped_in_what_the_read_only_commands_say`), the writers' refusals staying as they were; the `reset` half of the hostile-kind row is pinned by `togi::reset::a_hostile_unknown_kind_is_escaped_in_the_refusal_and_the_journal_is_left_alone`, added after the measurements. One test is newer than the inventory: the closure review found that `TestRunHardwareRestoresCursorAndPaletteWhenSessionPanics` had no Rust proof (the panic test used a no-op surface, the real-dashboard test ran `TERM=xterm` and ended on SIGINT), and added `togi::run::pty::a_session_panic_over_the_real_linux_console_dashboard_restores_the_cursor_and_the_palette` (the real dashboard on `TERM=linux`, a panic injected after the cursor is hidden and the palette set, then the cursor, palette, input settings and panic message are checked). That test is newer than `d1a4dd63`: the measurements in this entry do not include it, nor the tests #899 changed, nor that `reset` test. There are no Benchmark, Fuzz or Example functions.
- **The committed baseline stays Go (C3, [#538](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/538)).** [#878](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/878) adds `togi-tool-bench --baseline-out FILE` and `just bench-baseline-rs OUT`, which write the run's records without `model_check` and `wall_s`: 220 records in 168,505 bytes (the same run's full `--out` is 181,860), and 168,725 bytes on a rerun at the merged head, under the 200 kB the issue asks for. `tools/bench/baseline.jsonl` itself, the committed Go file, is not touched. It stays the Go file through the cutover ([#643](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/643)), because the Go bench's `--baseline`, the Go tests that pin it, `togi-tool-bench sanity` and the cutover's comparison of the Rust suite with Go's recorded scores all read it; it switches to a Rust recording in stage 9 ([#644](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/644)), when the Go bench is deleted.
- **Temporary journals (U2, [#613](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/613)).** [ADR 0060](adr/0060-bench-children-keep-temporary-journals.md) ([#882](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/882)): bench, `same`, `smoke` and `audit` children keep running with a temporary `--state-dir` and the parent reads and parses each journal, instead of the digest #613 decided. The measurements are in [#868](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/868): after #871 the child writes 5.04 MB instead of 347.6 MB for a default session, the dev split's 110 sessions took 27.0 s of wall time against 37.5 s, and an in-memory child cost no less. The rejected options are in the ADR.
- **The package ([#830](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/830), [#867](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/867)).** The flake gains `packages.togi-rs` and the check `rust-package` (`--version` and the four completion scripts); `packages.default` stays the Go build until the cutover. There are no Go tests to map: the change is the flake, a recipe and documentation, so the evidence is the pull request's log and body, recorded in [its entry](#stage-7-the-rust-package-867-2026-10-12). The Rust package's output is 1,360,000 B (closure 50,611,928 B) against the Go package's 12,690,568 B (53,058,064 B) at `3f3e78a7`, with only `--version`, `--help` and `completion` in the Rust binary then; CI's `rust-package` job took 107 s on `03e32522`. The darwin package was evaluated, not built.

**Host and method.** Stage 4's method on the development host: 32 threads, 30.9 GiB, Linux; `/tmp` is tmpfs and every build ran there, with the cargo target directory on disk. rustc and cargo 1.99.0, mold 2.42.1, cargo-nextest 0.9.146, go 1.27.1, hyperfine 1.21.0. A `git archive` of the revision, a private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` and Go build cache and `GOFLAGS=-mod=mod`, each crate and package built and tested once first, then every batch hyperfine `--warmup 1 --runs 10` with JSON export, the Rust and the Go command in one invocation, back to back; Go runs with `-tags integration`, as `just gate` does, so tagged tests count like Rust's integration tests. **Two revisions, each cohort at its own:** `togi-tool-modelcheck`, `togi-tool-forecast`, `togi-tool-facts`, `togi-tool-fit` and `togi-tool-stats` at `a3ccfe54` (`main` after #878), and `togi-tool-bench`, `togi-tool-audit` and `togi` at `d1a4dd63` (`main` after #880, every stage-7 pull request merged). The five crates and their Go packages are identical at the two revisions (their `git diff` is empty), so the first cohort is carried, not rerun; `togi-tool-audit` was not measured before the final revision. The host was loaded and shared: other agents' builds and tests ran throughout, and the one-minute load average at the batch boundaries was 5.2 to 51.9. The comparable figure is therefore CPU time, the mean user plus system time of the command and its children, which a busy host disturbs far less than wall time; wall time (median, with the minimum) is indicative only. Both cohorts are after [#860](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/860), which builds the measured crate itself at `opt-level = 1`, so these Rust figures are not comparable with stage 4's. **One batch was rerun.** In the first run of the final cohort the priming `cargo nextest run -p togi-tool-bench` failed one test of 285, `same::each_tree_is_built_and_its_own_simulator_run`, which failed the same way three times alone and so also failed that crate's test-time batch (no figure was kept for it from that run). The harness exports `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, which the test's fixture builds inherit, so its three trees share one target directory and the test's "same source in another directory" check fails; with the variable unset and the directory given to nextest as `--target-dir`, the test passes, as in CI, whose sandbox exports none. The batch was rerun alone, after the other batches, on the same tree and target directory: one nextest run of the whole crate with `--target-dir` and the variable unset (285 passed), then the same two hyperfine commands as before (`--warmup 1 --runs 10 -N`; the Rust command with `--target-dir`, the Go command through `env` for its cache and module variables), started at load 12.6 and ended at load 45.9. The `togi-tool-bench` test-time row, Go side included, is from that rerun; the other rows, and every compile batch, ran as in every other batch with the variable set, which does not change what compiles.

### Compile times

The crate or package alone, its dependencies built, with stage 4's commands (`cargo clean -p C` before a Rust run, a one-line new source file in the package before a Go run). CPU seconds, mean of 10 runs; "×" is Rust over Go:

| Crate / package | `cargo build` | `go build` | × | `nextest --no-run` | `go test -c` | × | `cargo build --release` |
|:---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `togi-tool-modelcheck` / `tools/modelcheck` | 0.54 | 0.14 | 3.9 | 2.48 | 0.44 | 5.7 | 0.31 |

Rust takes 2.9 to 15.2 times Go's CPU to compile a crate and 5.5 to 20.8 times to build its tests. The release build of a crate with a binary relinks the workspace with thin LTO, as stage 4 found for `togi-tool-sim`: the two libraries cost 0.31 and 1.08 s, the six crates with a binary 15 to 91 s. ### Test times

Everything built; hyperfine `-N`. Rust: `cargo nextest run -p C --locked` (a process per test). Go: `go test -tags integration -count=1 -timeout 5m ./P` (one process per package). CPU seconds, mean of 10 runs, and wall time, median (minimum), indicative only; the test counts are `cargo nextest list`'s and Go's test functions:

| Crate / package (Rust tests, Go tests) | nextest CPU | `go test` CPU | × CPU | nextest wall | `go test` wall |
|:---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `togi-tool-modelcheck` / `tools/modelcheck` (26, 11) | 0.24 | 0.20 | 1.2 | 0.14 (0.12) | 0.10 (0.09) |
| `togi-tool-forecast` / `tools/forecast` (75, 12) | 0.91 | 0.51 | 1.8 | 0.64 (0.61) | 0.27 (0.26) |
| `togi-tool-facts` / `tools/facts` (12, 3) | 0.15 | 0.16 | 0.9 | 0.11 (0.11) | 0.08 (0.08) |
| `togi-tool-fit` / `tools/fit` (61, 37) | 5.40 | 1.53 | 3.5 | 1.07 (1.04) | 1.08 (0.83) |

The Rust tests cost between 0.9 and 11.7 times Go's CPU: about the same for `facts` and `modelcheck`, 1.8 to 3.5 times for `forecast`, `fit` and `stats`, and 2.3 to 11.7 times for `audit`, `bench` and `togi`. `togi-tool-bench` runs the simulator and builds fixture trees with cargo (3.4 times), and `togi` is 11.7 times: its tests re-execute the binary and drive pseudo-terminals, signals and panics, which need a process each, against 118 Go functions that call functions in one process. Go's `tools/bench` wall time has a median of 6.46 s and a minimum of 2.83 s: that batch ran while the load average rose from 12.6 to 45.9, so read the minimum.

### Failures

`check.yml` on the sixteen pull requests' branches (`gh run list --workflow check.yml --branch B` and `gh run view --json jobs`; every run is a `pull_request` run): 73 runs, 43 successes, 6 failures and 24 cancelled, each cancelled run superseded by a newer push to the same branch; each cancelled run's aggregate `check` job reports `failure` only because the jobs it depends on were cancelled. Every pull request's final head passed. The supporting pull requests, counted apart: #860, #871 and #882 had 7 runs, 5 successes, 0 failures and 2 cancelled (#871's, superseded). Per pull request: #832 2 runs, #841 5 (1 failure), #844 8 (1 failure, 3 cancelled), #851 6 (2 cancelled), #852 3 (1 cancelled), #857 1, #861 4 (2 cancelled), #862 2 (1 failure), #866 4 (2 cancelled), #867 4, #869 9 (1 failure, 5 cancelled), #870 7 (1 failure, 2 cancelled), #872 8 (1 failure, 4 cancelled), #878 2 (1 cancelled), #879 4 (2 cancelled), #880 4.

- **A test that outran nextest's 120 s limit (2 failures).** #841 (`3913e441`) and #844 (`bcaf3c03`): `togi-session`'s `killing_the_run_at_every_durable_event_resumes_to_the_same_final_state` timed out in `rust-nextest`, a crate neither pull request touches. That is [#842](https://github.com/shgew/togi/issues/842), which [#845](https://github.com/shgew/togi/pull/845) fixed at 11:42Z; the next push of each, which merged `main`, passed.
- **The sandbox has no checkout's files (2 failures).** #862 (`e81a82df`): four `togi-tool-bench` tests failed in `rust-nextest`: three `gate_cli` tests ran the binary, which asks git for the revision, in the checkout, and the check's sandbox has no `.git`; and `gate`'s archive test read `tools/bench/gates`, which the flake's Rust source set did not include. #872 (`bafecbcb`): 14 `forecast` tests failed with `fatal: not a git repository`, the forecast library reading the tree's revision from the working directory. Both were also review P1 findings, fixed in #862 by `5049a123` and in #872 by `835b512e` (the command reads git and passes the tree in; tests run in a repository of their own).
- **clippy (2 failures).** #869 (`8276eaa6`, a work-in-progress push of review fixes): `rust-clippy` failed on a Linux-only test helper that was never used elsewhere (`session_start`, `dead_code` in the `cli` test target); `3105f63f` gates it to Linux. #870 (`f5a5bd3c`, also a work-in-progress push): an unused doc comment and `&Option<T>` instead of `Option<&T>` in `togi-tool-audit`; `406b4482` fixed both.
- Failures caught before a push by the local gate are recorded nowhere and are not measured. Failing jobs of other workflows are not counted.
